FR-387 Effective-profile resolution surface — Publish a resolution operation that, given a language/locale (and optional voice identity label and resolution hints), returns the single effective approved profile version and the reason it was selected. F-10 consumes this; F-16 does not speak, synthesize, or stream.
FR-388 Alignment re-run — Re-run preprocessing, alignment, embedding extraction, scoring and composition for a reference when samples, transcripts, or alignment settings change, producing a new candidate profile version without altering history.
FR-389 Drift monitoring — Periodically re-validate approved reference profiles against freshly supplied or re-sampled audio, and report drift (metric movement beyond configured tolerance) as a reviewable finding without auto-demoting the profile.
FR-390 Cross-sample consistency check — When a reference carries multiple samples of the same target voice, compare their embeddings and metrics and report consistency findings (outliers, mixed-speaker suspicion, recording-condition divergence).
FR-391 Search, filter and browse — Search references and profile versions by name, target voice label, locale, status, owner, tag, approval state, and verdict; filter by drift status and by effective/non-effective.
FR-392 Import and export — Export reference and profile-version metadata (and, where policy permits, embeddings) in a documented portable format, and import it into another installation or environment with idempotent re-keying and conflict reporting.
FR-393 Role-gated access — Enforce role-based permissions across reference registration, sample ingestion, alignment execution, approval, retirement, export, and deletion, with separation between the person who aligns and the person who approves where the configured policy requires it.
FR-394 Audit trail — Record every state-changing action on a reference, sample, alignment run, profile version, verdict, approval, binding, or retirement as an append-only auditable event with actor, timestamp, prior value, and new value.
FR-395 Alignment settings and presets — Provide named, versioned alignment settings presets (thresholds, canonical sample rate, normalization target, silence trim, channel policy, embedding model) that can be selected, cloned, and applied; every alignment run records the preset revision used.
FR-396 Batch alignment — Submit many references or samples for alignment as a batch, track per-item progress and outcome, and allow partial success without failing the whole batch.
FR-397 Failure diagnosis — For every failed or review sample, produce a machine-readable and human-readable diagnosis naming the failed stage, the metric that drove the verdict, and the most probable cause categories (silence-dominated, clipping, low speech duration, transcript mismatch, mixed speakers, unsupported format).
FR-398 Preview — Produce a preview artifact for a candidate profile version that demonstrates the aligned reference (for example a re-synthesized or re-rendered sample) so a reviewer can judge before approving, without that preview becoming the effective profile.
FR-399 Retirement and supersession — Retire a profile version while preserving its history, maintain an explicit supersession link to a successor, and refuse to treat a retired version as effective.
FR-400 Completion notifications and events — Emit a notification and a subscribable event when an alignment run, batch, drift check, or approval completes or fails, carrying the reference id, profile version, verdict summary, and a link to the authoritative record.

Non-Functional Requirements

ID Requirement
NFR-226 Alignment throughput — A single preprocessed reference sample of up to 10 minutes of speech must complete alignment, embedding extraction and scoring within 3 minutes of wall-clock time on the standard reference deployment profile.
NFR-227 Interactive latency — Registry reads, search, filtering, verdict retrieval and effective-profile resolution must return within 500 ms at the 95th percentile for catalogs up to 10,000 references.
NFR-228 Scalability and concurrency — Support at least 50 concurrent alignment jobs and 2,000 registered references with 20 profile versions each without degradation beyond the stated latency and throughput targets; batch submission must scale by bounded worker pools that never starve interactive reads.
NFR-229 Availability — The reference registry and effective-profile resolution surface must be available 99.9% monthly; a failure of the alignment worker tier must not take down registry reads or resolution of already-approved profiles.
NFR-230 Durability of reference assets — Ingested samples, preprocessed audio, embeddings, verdict bundles, profile versions and audit records must be stored with no loss across a single-node failure, and any lost artifact must be reconstructible from its recorded transformation chain.
NFR-231 Security of voice assets — Reference audio and embeddings are biometric-adjacent personal data: encrypt at rest and in transit, restrict access by role, never include raw audio or embeddings in logs or error payloads, and sign export bundles.
NFR-232 Consent and lawful-basis handling — Every reference must record the declared consent/lawful basis for the target voice and its samples; alignment and approval must be blocked when the required consent record is absent for the configured policy.
NFR-233 Authorization — Every operation must be authorized against the caller's scope; cross-tenant or cross-workspace access to another owner's references must be denied by default and auditable when denied.
NFR-234 Data residency — Reference audio, embeddings and derived artifacts must be placeable in a configured residency region and must not be replicated outside it by processing, preview, export, or notification paths.
NFR-235 Retention and deletion — Support configurable retention per reference and per sample; permanent deletion must remove raw audio, preprocessed audio, embeddings and derived previews, while preserving a tombstone audit event that records that deletion occurred.
NFR-236 Auditability and reproducibility — Every verdict, profile version and effective-profile resolution must be reproducible from stored inputs plus recorded settings revisions; the audit trail must be append-only and tamper-evident.
NFR-237 Deployment and environment portability — F-16 must function without outbound internet access (air-gapped/offline), must not require a specific vendor speech service, and must expose identical behaviour across the supported deployment options described by F-15.
NFR-238 Capacity and quota management — Enforce per-owner quotas on stored audio duration, embedding count and concurrent alignment jobs, and surface quota pressure before it blocks work.
NFR-239 Observability — Expose metrics for alignment queue depth, job duration by stage, verdict distribution, drift findings, quota usage and failure causes, and emit structured logs and traces correlated by alignment-run id.
NFR-240 Interoperability and contract stability — The published reference/profile/verdict/resolution contract must be machine-readable, versioned, additive-by-default, and usable through the uniform API conventions of F-13 without requiring dashboard access.

User Stories

ID Story
US-226 As a voice owner, I want to register a reference voice with its target locale, so that the companion has an authoritative voice identity to speak with.
US-227 As a voice owner, I want to upload several source samples for a reference, so that alignment has enough audio to build a faithful profile.
US-228 As a voice owner, I want the platform to normalize and align my samples automatically, so that I do not have to hand-edit audio before it is usable.
US-229 As a quality reviewer, I want an objective alignment score and verdict per sample, so that I can decide whether a reference is good enough to approve.
US-230 As a quality reviewer, I want a preview of a candidate profile before approving it, so that I can judge the voice rather than only the numbers.
US-231 As a quality reviewer, I want to see why a sample failed alignment, so that I can fix the input instead of guessing.
US-232 As an approver, I want an explicit approval step that records who approved which profile version, so that only intentionally-approved voices ever become effective.
US-233 As a platform operator, I want approved profiles bound to languages and locales, so that the companion resolves the right voice for the operator's locale.
US-234 As a platform operator, I want the effective-profile resolution to be explainable, so that I can answer "why is the companion speaking with this voice?"
US-235 As an operator, I want to re-run alignment after I add a better sample, so that the reference improves without losing its history.
US-236 As a platform operator, I want drift monitoring on approved profiles, so that a voice that degrades over time is surfaced for review before it surprises users.
US-237 As a compliance officer, I want a consent/lawful-basis record attached to every reference, so that we never align or approve a voice we are not allowed to use.
US-238 As a compliance officer, I want an immutable audit trail and residency controls, so that I can prove what happened to each voice asset and where it stayed.
US-239 As an automation engineer, I want to import/export references and drive alignment through the API, so that I can manage voices as code across environments.
US-240 As an administrator, I want role-gated control and quota enforcement over voice assets, so that a single team cannot exhaust capacity or approve its own voice without review.

Behaviour

  1. Registration. Creating a reference produces a draft record with no samples. The reference is not resolvable and can never be effective in this state. Registration captures target voice identity label, primary locale, owner, tags, and the consent/lawful-basis record (see business rules).
  2. Sample lifecycle. Each sample is ingested, then preprocessed, then aligned, then embedded, then scored — in that order. Each step writes its own artifact and status; a sample may be independently ingested, preprocessed, aligned, scored, failed, or excluded. Excluded samples remain visible with their exclusion reason and never contribute to a composed profile unless re-included.
  3. Alignment run. An alignment run is the unit that ties together a set of sample versions, one settings preset revision, and the produced candidate profile version. Runs are idempotent on (reference, sample version set, preset revision): re-submitting the same identity returns the existing run rather than producing a duplicate.
  4. Composition. A candidate profile is composed only from samples that reach scored. The composition strategy is recorded; multi-sample aggregation records the weighting used. A composition with zero eligible samples produces a failed run with a diagnosis, not an empty profile.
  5. Verdicts. Each sample verdict is pass, review, or fail. A profile-level verdict is derived from its contributing samples (for example, composed only of pass samples is pass; any review contributor is at best review; any fail contributor blocks composition with that strategy). Thresholds come from the preset revision recorded on the run.
  6. Approval. A reference's effective version pointer moves only through an explicit approval action on a specific profile version. Approval does not change the run's verdicts; it records the human decision separately.
  7. Binding and resolution. Locale bindings apply to an approved profile version. Resolution returns exactly one effective profile version per locale, or an explicit "unresolved" answer naming the competing or missing references. F-16 never picks a winner silently when a locale has conflicting approved candidates.
  8. Re-run and drift. Re-running alignment produces new sample versions, a new alignment run, and a new candidate profile version. Drift monitoring is a scheduled re-evaluation that emits a finding; it never auto-retires, auto-approves, or auto-rebinds anything.
  9. Retirement. Retiring a profile version leaves the version readable and auditable, removes it from resolution, and requires a supersession pointer when a successor exists. Retiring the currently-effective version for a locale leaves that locale explicitly unresolved until a successor is approved.
  10. Notifications and events. Completion events are emitted after the authoritative record is durably written, so a consumer that reacts to an event always finds the referenced record present.

Business Rules

ID Rule
BR-1 A reference must have a consent/lawful-basis record before any sample may be aligned; absence blocks alignment with a specific, itemized error.
BR-2 Only a profile version in approved state may become effective; draft, in-review, retired, and failed versions are never resolvable.
BR-3 Profile versions are immutable. Any change — new sample, new preset, new transcript — produces a new version that points at its predecessor.
BR-4 Historical alignment runs, verdicts, and audit events are never edited or deleted except by explicit, audited retention deletion.
BR-5 At most one approved profile version per (reference, locale) may be effective at a time; a second approval for the same locale requires retiring or re-binding the first.
BR-6 Effective-profile resolution must be explainable: it always states the reference, profile version, locale, and selection reason.
BR-7 Where policy requires separation of duties, the identity that triggered alignment or composition of a profile version may not be the identity that approves it.
BR-8 Verdicts are reproducible: they are stored with the metric values and the exact settings-preset revision that produced them; re-deriving a verdict for an old run must use that stored revision.
BR-9 Drift findings, consistency findings, and low-confidence verdicts are reviewable queue items; they never change effective state on their own.
BR-10 Raw audio and embeddings are biometric-adjacent assets: they are excluded from logs, telemetry payloads, error bodies, and notification payloads.
BR-11 Export bundles must be signed and must respect residency and consent scope; an export that would cross a residency boundary is refused, not silently redacted.
BR-12 Deleting a reference permanently removes its audio, embeddings and previews but leaves an immutable tombstone audit event stating that deletion occurred.
BR-13 Quotas on stored duration, embedding count and concurrent jobs are enforced before work is accepted, not after it has consumed resources.

Validation

ID Validation
V-1 Reference name is required, non-empty, and unique within its owner/workspace scope.
V-2 Primary locale must be a registered language/locale tag; alternate locales must each be registered tags.
V-3 Target voice identity label is required and must be a permitted value for the configured policy.
V-4 A consent/lawful-basis record reference is required before alignment is permitted (see BR-1).
V-5 An ingested sample must decode successfully; unsupported codec or corrupt payload is rejected at ingestion with the detected problem.
V-6 A sample must declare or be assigned a language that matches the reference's declared locale set; a mismatch is a validation warning that becomes a blocking error only when the preset requires strict locale matching.
V-7 A sample must contain at least the configured minimum effective speech duration after preprocessing; shorter samples are marked review or fail per preset, never silently accepted.
V-8 A declared transcript, when present, must be non-empty and must be internally consistent in language with the sample declaration.
V-9 Sample rate, channel count and bit depth must be within the supported ranges of the selected preset; out-of-range inputs are rejected or converted only when the preset permits conversion.
V-10 Settings presets must be internally consistent (for example, a silence-trim threshold larger than the minimum speech duration is rejected).
V-11 A locale binding must reference an approved profile version; binding a non-approved version is rejected.
V-12 A retirement request naming a successor must reference an existing, non-retired profile version of the same reference.
V-13 Batch submissions must not exceed the configured batch size or the owner's concurrent-job quota.
V-14 Import payloads must declare the format version and pass schema and signature validation before any record is written.

Edge Cases

ID Edge case
EC-1 Silence-dominated sample: preprocessing leaves almost no speech; alignment must mark the sample fail with a silence-dominated diagnosis rather than producing a degenerate embedding.
EC-2 Clipped or heavily distorted sample: clipping ratio above the preset threshold produces review/fail with a distortion diagnosis, and the sample is never silently included in a composed profile.
EC-3 Mixed speakers in one sample: consistency check reports a mixed-speaker suspicion; composition excludes it unless the preset explicitly permits mixed-source material.
EC-4 Transcript mismatch: alignment confidence falls below threshold across the sample; the run reports transcript-mismatch as the probable cause and the sample is excluded.
EC-5 Multiple approved references claiming the same locale: resolution returns an explicit conflict naming both references instead of silently selecting one.
EC-6 Effective profile retired with no successor: the locale is left unresolved and the companion's consumer (F-10) is expected to fall back to its own defined degradation; F-16 states the locale is unresolved.
EC-7 Duplicate submission of the same alignment run: idempotency returns the existing run and does not create a second profile version.
EC-8 Concurrent re-run while an approval is in progress: the approval binds to the version it was opened against; the newly produced successor does not silently inherit the in-flight approval.
EC-9 Sample added after a profile version was approved: the approved version is unchanged; the new sample only affects a future successor version.
EC-10 Very long sample exceeding the configured maximum duration: the sample is rejected or split according to preset policy, and the effective action is recorded.
EC-11 Locale with no approved profile at all: resolution returns "unresolved — no approved reference" rather than an error, so F-10 can degrade gracefully.
EC-12 Drift detected while the profile is concurrently under review: the drift finding attaches to the version under review and does not create a competing version.
EC-13 Export requested for a reference whose consent scope forbids export: refusal is explicit and audited; nothing partial is produced.
EC-14 Deletion requested for a reference that backs a currently-effective locale: deletion is refused until the locale is re-bound or the reference is retired through the normal workflow.
EC-15 Air-gapped deployment: alignment, scoring, resolution and export must all work offline; no external speech service may be required.

Error Handling

ID Error Handling
EH-1 Missing consent/lawful basis Reject alignment with a consent_required error naming the reference and the missing record; no partial artifacts are produced.
EH-2 Undecodable sample Reject at ingestion with sample_undecodable, including the detected codec/container problem; the reference remains usable with its other samples.
EH-3 Alignment engine failure Mark the alignment run failed, preserve all successfully produced artifacts, emit a completion event with the failure, and allow a re-run that reuses prior successful stages where the preset permits.
EH-4 Threshold violation producing fail Not an error condition of the platform — it is a stored verdict with diagnosis; it blocks composition with that strategy and is reported, not thrown.
EH-5 Locale conflict at resolution Return an unresolved_conflict result that names the competing references and versions; callers (for example F-10) treat this as a defined, degradable state, not an exception.
EH-6 Unauthorized operation Return an unauthorized error without disclosing the existence of the resource; record the denial in the audit trail.
EH-7 Quota exceeded Return quota_exceeded before accepting the work, naming the exceeded dimension and the current usage; queued work is not silently dropped.
EH-8 Storage or durability failure during artifact write Fail the run, do not advance the version, and ensure no partial profile version is marked resolvable; retry per the platform retry policy.
EH-9 Residency violation attempt (processing, export, notification) Refuse with residency_violation and audit the attempt; never silently route around the boundary.
EH-10 Import schema/signature failure Reject the whole import atomically with the failing item identified; no partial records are written.
EH-11 Notification/event publish failure Do not lose the authoritative record; retry publication and, on exhaustion, surface the undelivered event as an observable backlog item.
EH-12 Deletion of a reference still in effective use Refuse with in_use naming the locale bindings that must be resolved first.

Acceptance Criteria

ID Criterion
AC-1 A newly registered reference with no samples is never resolvable and never appears as an effective profile for any locale.
AC-2 Ingesting a valid sample and running alignment produces a scored sample plus a candidate profile version, with the settings-preset revision recorded on the run.
AC-3 Every verdict is stored together with its metric values and preset revision, and re-deriving the verdict from those stored values yields the same verdict.
AC-4 A profile version cannot become effective without an explicit approval action that records approver, timestamp, and decision note.
AC-5 Approving two profile versions for the same (reference, locale) is rejected unless the earlier one is retired or re-bound.
AC-6 Effective-profile resolution returns exactly one version per locale, or an explicit unresolved result naming the reason — never a silent guess.
AC-7 Re-running alignment after adding a sample creates a new profile version and leaves the previously approved version byte-identical.
AC-8 Drift monitoring produces a reviewable finding and never changes effective state on its own.
AC-9 A failed sample reports a diagnosis naming the failed stage, the driving metric, and a probable-cause category.
AC-10 Missing consent blocks alignment with a consent_required error and no artifacts.
AC-11 Role separation, where configured, prevents the aligning identity from approving the same profile version.
AC-12 Every state-changing action appears in the append-only audit trail with actor, timestamp, prior value and new value.
AC-13 Raw audio and embeddings never appear in logs, telemetry, error bodies, or notification payloads.
AC-14 Export of a reference whose consent scope forbids export is refused and audited, producing no partial bundle.
AC-15 Permanent deletion removes audio, embeddings and previews while leaving a tombstone audit event.
AC-16 All alignment, scoring, resolution and export operations complete successfully in an air-gapped environment with no external speech service.
AC-17 An alignment run, batch, drift check, or approval emits a completion event only after the authoritative record is durably written.
AC-18 Reference and profile data can be exported and re-imported into another environment with idempotent re-keying and an explicit conflict report.

API Behaviour

ID Endpoint / operation Behaviour
API-1 POST /voice-references Create a reference. Returns the reference record with status: draft and a stable id. Validates name, locales, target voice label and consent reference (V-1, V-2, V-3, V-4).
API-2 GET /voice-references/{id} and GET /voice-references Read one or list, with filtering by status, locale, owner, tag, verdict, effective flag, drift status (FR-391). Supports pagination and sparse fieldsets per the F-13 envelope conventions.
API-3 PATCH /voice-references/{id} Update mutable reference metadata (name, description, tags, locale declarations) while preserving history; status changes flow only through the lifecycle operations below.
API-4 POST /voice-references/{id}/samples Ingest a sample. Returns the sample record with ingestion metadata and status ingested. Rejects undecodable or out-of-range input (V-5, V-9, EH-2).
API-5 GET /voice-references/{id}/samples List samples with per-sample status, verdict, and diagnosis summary.
API-6 POST /voice-references/{id}/alignment-runs Start a run. Accepts a sample-version set, a preset revision, and an optional idempotency key. Returns a run id immediately; idempotent on (reference, sample-version set, preset revision) (EC-7).
API-7 GET /voice-references/{id}/alignment-runs/{runId} Return run status, per-sample stage progress, produced profile version, and failure diagnosis (FR-397).
API-8 GET /voice-references/{id}/profile-versions List immutable profile versions with predecessor pointers, contributing sample versions, composition strategy, and verdict bundle.
API-9 GET /voice-references/{id}/profile-versions/{version} Return the full profile version including verdicts, metric values, and preset revision for reproducibility (BR-8).
API-10 POST /voice-references/{id}/profile-versions/{version}/preview Produce or fetch a preview artifact for the candidate; the preview never becomes effective (FR-398).
API-11 POST /voice-references/{id}/profile-versions/{version}/approve Approve a specific version, recording approver, note, and the verdict bundle. Enforces separation of duties where configured (BR-7, AC-11) and the single-effective-per-locale rule (BR-5, AC-5).
API-12 POST /voice-references/{id}/profile-versions/{version}/retire Retire a version with an optional supersession pointer; enforces successor validity (V-12) and leaves bound locales explicitly unresolved if no successor is effective (EC-6).
API-13 PUT /voice-reference-bindings/{locale} Bind an approved profile version to a locale; rejects binding a non-approved version (V-11). Conflicts across references are surfaced by resolution, not resolved here.
API-14 GET /voice-reference-resolution Resolve the effective approved profile for a locale (and optional voice label/hints). Returns exactly one version with a selection reason, or an explicit unresolved result (FR-387, BR-6, EH-5, EC-11). This is the surface F-10 consumes.
API-15 POST /voice-reference-drift-checks Schedule or trigger a drift check for an approved profile version; returns a finding record and never changes effective state (FR-389, BR-9).
API-16 POST /voice-references/{id}/alignment-batches Submit a batch; returns a batch id and per-item tracking, and supports partial success (FR-396, V-13).
API-17 POST /voice-references/export and POST /voice-references/import Produce a signed export bundle or consume one. Refuses on residency or consent violation (EH-9, EC-13) and rejects invalid imports atomically (V-14, EH-10).
API-18 DELETE /voice-references/{id} Permanently delete a reference and its audio, embeddings and previews, leaving a tombstone audit event; refuses while the reference backs an effective locale (FR-399, EH-12, AC-15).
API-19 GET /voice-references/{id}/audit-events Return the append-only audit trail for the reference, filterable by event type and actor (FR-394, AC-12).
API-20 POST /voice-reference-settings-presets and GET /voice-reference-settings-presets Create, clone, version and read alignment settings presets; rejects internally inconsistent presets (V-10).

Envelope and cross-cutting API notes: all operations use the uniform request/response envelope, error shape, pagination, filtering, sparse fieldsets, idempotency and concurrency-control conventions owned by F-13; F-16 does not define a competing envelope. Every operation is authorized against the caller's scope (NFR-233) and every state-changing operation is audited (FR-394). Publishing the reference/profile/verdict/resolution contract as a machine-readable, versioned, additive-by-default surface (NFR-240) is delegated to the contract machinery of F-13; F-16 supplies the operations and schemas. The API is read/write for management and read-only for consumption: F-10 resolves and reads profiles, and never writes alignment or approval state on F-16's behalf.

Priority

Must-have (release-critical): FR-376 registry, FR-377 sample ingestion, FR-378 preprocessing/normalization, FR-379 phonetic/transcript alignment, FR-380 embedding extraction, FR-381 alignment scoring, FR-382 verdicts and thresholds, FR-383 profile composition, FR-384 immutable versioning, FR-385 approval workflow, FR-386 locale binding, FR-387 effective-profile resolution surface, FR-388 alignment re-run, FR-393 role-gated access, FR-394 audit trail, FR-395 settings presets, FR-397 failure diagnosis, FR-399 retirement and supersession; NFR-226, NFR-229, NFR-230, NFR-231, NFR-232, NFR-233, NFR-234, NFR-236, NFR-240.

Should-have: FR-389 drift monitoring, FR-390 cross-sample consistency check, FR-391 search/filter/browse, FR-392 import/export, FR-396 batch alignment, FR-398 preview, FR-400 completion notifications and events; NFR-227, NFR-228, NFR-235, NFR-237, NFR-238, NFR-239.

Nice-to-have: advanced preset authoring conveniences and additional non-blocking reporting refinements on top of the above (for example richer browse analytics); no additional global IDs are introduced for these.

Open question for the USER (not decided here): whether F-16 should ultimately support voice cloning / synthesis of a target speaker from the reference as part of the alignment deliverable, or whether the aligned reference is strictly a speaker-identity/quality artifact consumed by F-10's chosen synthesis path. This spec implements the latter, non-generative interpretation (alignment, scoring, approval, resolution, preview) and does not add cloning capabilities; if generative voice cloning is required, that is a scope decision belonging to the user.

Functional Requirements

The following is the master functional-requirements catalog for Product Forge. Each FR id is globally unique and owned by exactly one feature. Requirements are grouped by feature; each feature's FRs are also restated in that feature's own ## F-<n> section.

F-1: Central Pipeline Control Plane

  • FR-1 — Project lifecycle registry. Create, read, list, update, archive, restore, and permanently delete pipeline projects. Every project carries name, description, owner, portfolio membership, status, pipeline binding, and configuration revision.
  • FR-2 — Portfolio grouping and roll-up. Create and manage portfolios; assign/unassign projects; present aggregate state across member projects (run counts by status, gate backlog, failure counts, last activity).
  • FR-3 — Pipeline definition and version pinning. Register pipeline definitions composed of ordered stages; pin each project to an immutable pipeline version; allow explicit upgrade to a newer version without mutating historical runs.
  • FR-4 — Run execution control. Start, pause, resume, cancel, and retry pipeline runs. Control commands apply to the whole run or to a specified stage, and are recorded as auditable commands.
  • FR-5 — Stage orchestration. Drive runs through the registered 32-stage model: resolve stage order, evaluate entry conditions, dispatch work, capture stage outcome, and transition to the next stage.
  • FR-6 — Project metadata and status management. Maintain project status transitions (draft/active/paused/archived) and all descriptive metadata, with change history.
  • FR-7 — Project search, filter, and sort. Query the registry by name, owner, portfolio, status, pipeline version, tags, and last activity, with stable sorting and pagination.
  • FR-8 — Archive/restore/delete semantics. Soft-archive preserves history; restore reinstates; permanent delete is guarded, confirmed, and audited.
  • FR-9 — Run history and audit trail. Retain an immutable record of every run, command, and state transition with actor, timestamp, and outcome.
  • FR-10 — Pipeline definition registry. Register, version, and retire pipeline definitions composed of ordered stages; validate stage references.
  • FR-11 — Stage registry. Expose the canonical stage catalog (id, name, order, entry/exit conditions, gate policy) as immutable, versioned metadata.
  • FR-12 — Run telemetry capture. Persist per-stage telemetry (start/end, duration, outcome, logs reference, artifacts reference) for every run.
  • FR-13 — Configuration revision management. Track and expose per-project configuration revisions; every mutation increments the revision.
  • FR-14 — Registry authorization and ownership. Enforce owner/role-based permissions for registry reads and mutations.
  • FR-15 — Registry events and notifications. Emit registry lifecycle events (project/portfolio/run created, updated, archived) to subscribers.
  • FR-16 — Bulk project operations. Apply archive/restore/assign/reassign operations to a set of projects atomically or with per-item results.
  • FR-17 — Registry idempotency and concurrency control. Guarantee idempotent mutations via idempotency keys and reject stale writes via revision checks.
  • FR-18 — Registry observability and health. Expose health, latency, and error metrics for registry operations.
  • FR-19 — Registry data export/import. Export projects, portfolios, and bindings in a machine-readable format; import with validation and conflict reporting.
  • FR-20 — Pipeline version upgrade governance. Require explicit, audited upgrade actions; block silent version drift; surface impact of upgrade before applying.

F-2: Project Creation and Model Tier Management

  • FR-26 — Project creation wizard entry. Provide a guided, multi-step entry point to create a new pipeline project, launched from the control plane (F-1) and resumable across sessions.
  • FR-27 — Idea capture. Capture the seed idea as free-form text, with optional structured fields (goal, target users, success signals); attach it to the project draft.
  • FR-28 — Project metadata. Collect and validate the project's name, description, owner, tags, and visibility during creation.
  • FR-29 — Portfolio assignment at creation. Allow assignment to zero or one portfolio at creation, with inline creation of a new portfolio.
  • FR-30 — Pipeline definition binding. Let the operator select a pipeline definition and pinned version; default to the platform default definition when none is selected.
  • FR-31 — Model tier selection. Allow the operator to pick a predefined model tier (e.g., economical/balanced/frontier) as the project's default.
  • FR-32 — Model tier registry. Maintain the catalog of predefined model tiers with their model/provider composition, cost/quality profile, and availability.
  • FR-33 — Custom model tier creation. Allow the operator to compose a new tier from the catalog of LLM models and providers, naming and describing it.
  • FR-34 — Model/provider catalog. Expose the registered LLM models and providers with capabilities, context limits, and regional availability.
  • FR-35 — Tier editing and versioning. Editing a tier produces a new immutable version; existing projects pinned to an older version are unaffected until explicitly upgraded.
  • FR-36 — Tier validation and compatibility. Validate that every tier's models/providers exist, are available, and satisfy pipeline stage constraints.
  • FR-37 — Draft persistence and resume. Persist in-progress creation drafts and allow resuming from the last completed step.
  • FR-38 — Creation validation and readiness. Validate all required fields and bindings before allowing completion; present an itemized readiness report.
  • FR-39 — Duplicate/clone project. Create a new project by cloning an existing project's metadata, bindings, and tier (excluding run history).
  • FR-40 — Creation authorization and quota. Enforce who may create projects, custom tiers, and portfolios, and enforce any configured quotas.
  • FR-41 — Creation idempotency. Prevent duplicate project/tier creation from retried submissions via idempotency keys.
  • FR-42 — Tier defaults and inheritance. Apply platform/portfolio default tiers where the operator does not choose, and expose which default applied.
  • FR-43 — Tier change governance on existing projects. Changing a project's tier requires an explicit, audited action; historical runs retain their original tier.
  • FR-44 — Creation audit trail. Record who created/modified projects, tiers, and drafts, when, and with what inputs.
  • FR-45 — Creation notifications. Notify relevant subscribers on project/tier creation, edit, or failure.

F-3: E2E Pipeline Initiation and Monitoring

  • FR-51 — Run initiation from project. Start a run for an existing project bound to an immutable pipeline version; accept optional idempotency key, run label, and starting-stage override (only where entry conditions permit). Return a stable run id immediately.
  • FR-52 — Pre-flight readiness validation. Before any stage executes, validate seed idea, required metadata, resolved model tier, bound pipeline definition/version, and gate policy; block with an itemized readiness report on failure.
  • FR-53 — Run plan materialization. Expand the pinned pipeline version into a concrete, ordered run plan of stages with resolved parameters.
  • FR-54 — Stage dispatch and execution. Execute each stage in plan order, honoring entry conditions and dispatching the appropriate stage worker/agent.
  • FR-55 — Live progress and telemetry streaming. Stream run and stage progress (state, percent, current activity, logs, metrics) to subscribed clients in near real time.
  • FR-56 — HIL gate pause and decision. Pause at each human-in-the-loop gate, present the decision context, and resume based on the recorded decision.
  • FR-57 — Run completion and outcome. Finalize a run with a terminal outcome (succeeded/failed/cancelled) and persist the complete stage outcomes.
  • FR-58 — Failure handling and retry. Detect stage failures, classify them, and support retry per policy or explicit command without corrupting prior records.
  • FR-59 — Run cancellation. Cancel a run gracefully, stopping in-flight work and marking remaining stages as cancelled.
  • FR-60 — Run pause/resume. Pause a run at the next safe point and resume from the exact recorded state.
  • FR-61 — Stage retry/skip. Retry or skip an individual stage where policy permits, recording the action and its effect.
  • FR-62 — Run audit record. Retain an immutable, ordered audit record of all run/stage events with actors and timestamps.
  • FR-63 — Run state persistence and recovery. Persist run/stage state so a restart resumes or safely fails-forward without losing or duplicating work.
  • FR-64 — Run idempotency. Ensure a repeated initiation/command with the same idempotency key does not create duplicate runs or effects.
  • FR-65 — Run concurrency control. Enforce configured per-project and global concurrency limits, queuing or rejecting excess runs.
  • FR-66 — Stage entry/exit conditions. Evaluate and enforce each stage's declared entry and exit conditions before and after execution.
  • FR-67 — Run notifications and subscriptions. Emit run/stage/gate events to in-app, email, and webhook subscribers.
  • FR-68 — Run observability. Expose run-level metrics, structured logs, and tracing correlated by run/stage id.
  • FR-69 — Run log and artifact retention. Store run logs and artifacts per retention policy with retrievable references.
  • FR-70 — Run role-based authorization. Enforce who may initiate, view, and control runs.

F-4: Portfolio Management

  • FR-76 — Portfolio registry and lifecycle. Create, read, list, update, archive, restore, and delete portfolios with name, description, owner, and status.
  • FR-77 — Portfolio membership management. Assign, unassign, and move projects between portfolios, with rules preventing orphaned or ambiguous membership.
  • FR-78 — Portfolio roll-up aggregation. Compute always-current aggregates across member projects: run counts by status, gate backlog, failure counts, capacity pressure, and last activity.
  • FR-79 — Scoped bulk operations. Apply archive/pause/resume/run-initiation operations to all or a filtered subset of member projects, with per-item results.
  • FR-80 — Portfolio run and gate views. Present runs, stages, and pending HIL gates for all member projects in one consolidated view (governed by F-3 data).
  • FR-81 — Portfolio capacity pressure. Surface concurrency/queue pressure across member projects relative to configured limits.
  • FR-82 — Portfolio failure and last-activity tracking. Highlight failing projects/runs and the most recent activity per project.
  • FR-83 — Portfolio authorization scope. Enforce who may view or mutate a portfolio and exercise scoped bulk operations.
  • FR-84 — Portfolio reporting surface. Provide exportable portfolio reports (state summary, run outcomes, gate backlog) over a chosen time window.
  • FR-85 — Portfolio idempotency/concurrency. Guarantee idempotent mutations and reject stale updates via revision checks.
  • FR-86 — Portfolio notifications. Notify on membership changes, bulk-operation completion, and threshold breaches.
  • FR-87 — Portfolio audit trail. Record all portfolio mutations and scoped commands immutably.
  • FR-88 — Portfolio search/filter. Query portfolios by name, owner, status, and size.
  • FR-89 — Portfolio health/observability. Expose metrics for roll-up freshness, aggregation latency, and errors.
  • FR-90 — Portfolio data export. Export portfolio metadata and roll-up snapshots in a machine-readable format.

F-5: Multi-Project Runs

  • FR-101 — Multi-project run group creation. Create a durable parent run group targeting multiple projects, with name, owner, optional portfolio context, scope definition, execution mode, failure policy, shared parameters, and idempotency key.
  • FR-102 — Scope resolution. Resolve the target project set from explicit ids, portfolio membership, tags, or filters, capturing an immutable scope snapshot.
  • FR-103 — Child run materialization. Create one child pipeline run per resolved project, applying the run initiation and pre-flight rules of F-3.
  • FR-104 — Shared and overridden parameters. Apply group-level shared parameters with per-project overrides where permitted.
  • FR-105 — Execution policy. Enforce group execution mode: parallel fan-out, staged waves, or serialized, within configured concurrency limits.
  • FR-106 — Failure policy. Apply fail-fast, continue-on-error, or threshold-based abort across child runs per the group's policy.
  • FR-107 — Aggregated monitoring. Present aggregate and per-child progress, outcomes, and telemetry for the group.
  • FR-108 — Aggregated HIL gates. Consolidate pending human-in-the-loop gates across child runs into a single group gate view for decision.
  • FR-109 — Parent-child audit trail. Retain a complete, immutable audit trail linking the parent group to every child run, command, and outcome.
  • FR-110 — Group lifecycle control. Pause, resume, cancel, and retry the group, propagating to child runs where policy permits.
  • FR-111 — Group idempotency. Prevent duplicate groups or duplicate child runs from retried submissions.
  • FR-112 — Group concurrency control. Enforce concurrency limits across all in-flight child runs.
  • FR-113 — Group notifications. Emit progress and completion notifications aggregated per group and per child.
  • FR-114 — Group authorization. Enforce who may create, view, and control multi-project run groups.
  • FR-115 — Group observability. Expose group-level metrics, logs, and tracing correlated by group id.

F-6: Manual Input and Operation Control

  • FR-126 — Manual input supply. Allow an operator to supply input to a running or paused run/stage that requests human input.
  • FR-127 — HIL gate decision. Allow an operator to approve, reject, or conditionally decide a pending human-in-the-loop gate with a rationale.
  • FR-128 — Run/stage pause/resume/cancel. Issue pause, resume, and cancel commands to a run or to a specified stage.
  • FR-129 — Stage retry/skip. Retry or skip a specified stage where policy permits, recording the action and effect.
  • FR-130 — Parameter override. Override run/stage parameters within the bounds permitted by policy, with the change recorded and effect visible.
  • FR-131 — Emergency system-wide halt. Provide an emergency halt that stops all active runs immediately, with a reason and audit record.
  • FR-132 — Preview/dry-run. Preview the effect of a command (targets, param diffs, predicted impact) before it is applied.
  • FR-133 — Confirmation. Require explicit confirmation for destructive or high-impact commands.
  • FR-134 — Command idempotency. Guarantee that a retried command with the same idempotency key is applied at most once.
  • FR-135 — Command conflict handling. Detect and resolve conflicts with concurrent commands or stale run state, failing safely.
  • FR-136 — Command audit. Record every manual command with actor, target, input, preview, and outcome immutably.
  • FR-137 — Role gating. Restrict command classes to authorized roles and scopes.
  • FR-138 — Bulk manual commands. Apply a command class to a selected set of runs/stages with per-item results.
  • FR-139 — Command notifications. Notify stakeholders on command acceptance, completion, and failure.
  • FR-140 — Command authorization scope. Enforce project/portfolio/run scoping for all command issuance.

F-7: Auto Mode

  • FR-151 — Auto mode policy definition. Define a declarative, versioned Auto Mode policy (autonomy level, gate rules, retry/abort rules, guardrails).
  • FR-152 — Autonomy level binding. Bind an autonomy level (assist / supervised-auto / full-auto) to a project, run, or run group.
  • FR-153 — Automatic HIL gate decisions. Evaluate HIL gates defined by F-3 against policy and auto-decide where policy permits, escalating when not.
  • FR-154 — Auto-retry/skip/pause/halt/escalate. Automatically retry, skip, pause, halt, or escalate according to policy triggers.
  • FR-155 — Guardrails. Enforce budget, time, and risk guardrails, halting or escalating on breach.
  • FR-156 — Bounded automatic parameter overrides. Apply automatic parameter overrides only within explicitly bounded ranges.
  • FR-157 — Decision ledger. Maintain an immutable, explainable ledger of every automatic decision with the rule and inputs that produced it.
  • FR-158 — Kill switch / manual takeover. Provide an immediate kill switch that disables auto mode and hands control to F-6.
  • FR-159 — Fail-closed evaluation. Escalate rather than guess when policy is ambiguous or unknown.
  • FR-160 — Policy versioning. Version auto-mode policies; changes do not retroactively alter past decisions.
  • FR-161 — Scope binding. Bind policies at project, portfolio, or run-group scope with clear precedence.
  • FR-162 — Auto mode notifications. Notify on escalations, guardrail breaches, and auto-decisions requiring attention.
  • FR-163 — Auto mode audit. Record policy changes and all auto-mode actions immutably.
  • FR-164 — Auto mode authorization. Enforce who may define, bind, or disable auto-mode policies.
  • FR-165 — Auto mode observability. Expose metrics for auto-decisions, escalations, and guardrail events.

F-8: Dashboard UX Component System

  • FR-176 — Design token foundation. Provide a versioned design-token set (color, spacing, typography, elevation, motion) consumed by all components.
  • FR-177 — Primitive components. Deliver the primitive library (buttons, inputs, tags, icons, tooltips, etc.).
  • FR-178 — Composed components. Deliver composed patterns (forms, tables, dialogs, tabs, breadcrumbs, banners).
  • FR-179 — Feature components. Deliver feature components (run timeline, stage card, gate panel, portfolio roll-up, telemetry chart).
  • FR-180 — Loading states. Provide skeletons and progress affordances for content and long operations.
  • FR-181 — Empty states. Provide empty-state patterns with guidance and CTAs for every list/panel.
  • FR-182 — Error states. Provide inline, toast, and page-level error presentations with recovery actions.
  • FR-183 — Real-time update behaviours. Standardize how live telemetry updates render without disruptive layout shifts.
  • FR-184 — Theming. Support light/dark (and additional) themes via tokens without component forks.
  • FR-185 — Responsiveness. Provide responsive layouts from mobile to desktop breakpoints.
  • FR-186 — Accessibility. Meet WCAG 2.1 AA: contrast, keyboard operability, focus visibility, ARIA, and screen-reader semantics.
  • FR-187 — Internationalization. Support localized strings, RTL, and locale-aware number/date/currency formatting.
  • FR-188 — Motion. Provide consistent, reduced-motion-respecting animation guidance.
  • FR-189 — Pipeline-state presentation contract. Define how project/run/stage states are color-coded, labelled, and announced consistently.
  • FR-190 — Telemetry presentation contract. Define how run/telemetry data is visualized consistently across surfaces.
  • FR-191 — Command presentation contract. Define consistent affordances, confirmation, and result feedback for operator commands (semantics owned by F-6).
  • FR-192 — Component versioning. Version components and tokens with documented migration paths.
  • FR-193 — Component documentation. Provide usage docs, examples, and accessibility notes for every component.

F-9: Global AI Chat Companion

  • FR-201 — Availability. Provide an always-reachable chat companion openable from any dashboard surface.
  • FR-202 — Context binding. Automatically bind the conversation to the operator's current context (project, run, stage, gate, portfolio, run group, auto-mode session).
  • FR-203 — Grounded Q&A. Answer questions about authoritative pipeline state by reading published surfaces of other features.
  • FR-204 — Citations. Cite the authoritative object behind every factual statement about pipeline state.
  • FR-205 — Explanation and diagnosis. Explain behaviour and diagnose failures using authoritative state.
  • FR-206 — Command drafting. Draft commands for the operator without executing them.
  • FR-207 — Hand-off to command surface. Pass drafted commands to the F-6 manual command surface for preview and confirmation.
  • FR-208 — Refusal to invent. Refuse to invent state, commands, or capabilities; state clearly when information is unavailable.
  • FR-209 — Authorization scope enforcement. Never expose data or draft commands beyond the operator's authorization scope.
  • FR-210 — Graceful degradation. Degrade to deterministic help and search when the AI backend is unavailable.
  • FR-211 — Help and search fallback. Provide navigable help topics and search when AI is unavailable.
  • FR-212 — Conversation history. Retain scoped, retrievable conversation history honoring privacy controls.
  • FR-213 — Feedback. Allow operators to rate answers and flag incorrect grounding.
  • FR-214 — Internationalization. Respond in the operator's configured language.
  • FR-215 — Accessibility. Be fully keyboard- and screen-reader-operable.
  • FR-216 — Observability. Expose metrics for grounding accuracy, refusal rate, latency, and errors.
  • FR-217 — Privacy controls. Provide controls over conversation retention and data sent to the AI backend.

F-10: Voice Companion

  • FR-226 — Activation and microphone lifecycle. Provide push-to-talk and/or wake-word activation with an explicit, revocable microphone lifecycle.
  • FR-227 — Streaming speech-to-text. Transcribe speech to text in a streaming manner with interim and final results.
  • FR-228 — Spoken synthesis. Speak grounded answers synthesized from the F-9 response.
  • FR-229 — Spoken citations. Verbally attribute statements to their authoritative objects.
  • FR-230 — Barge-in. Allow the operator to interrupt playback and speak; the system stops and listens.
  • FR-231 — Hands-free HIL gate announcements. Announce pending HIL gates aloud and accept a spoken decision, routed through F-6 for confirmation/audit.
  • FR-232 — Voice-driven navigation. Navigate dashboard surfaces by voice.
  • FR-233 — Voice drafting of commands. Capture a spoken command and hand it to F-6 as a draft for preview/confirmation.
  • FR-234 — Multilingual speech. Support multiple speech locales for recognition and synthesis.
  • FR-235 — Transcript/audio privacy controls. Provide controls over transcript/audio retention and sharing.
  • FR-236 — Graceful degradation to text. Fall back to the text companion when voice services are unavailable.
  • FR-237 — Voice permission management. Request, display, and honor microphone permissions; never record without consent.
  • FR-238 — Session control. Provide explicit start/stop and a visible listening indicator.
  • FR-239 — Voice observability. Expose metrics for recognition latency, synthesis latency, and failures.
  • FR-240 — Voice accessibility. Ensure voice features do not gate access and always have operable text alternatives.

F-11: Mobile Dashboard Companion

  • FR-251 — Responsive read views. Render the authoritative pipeline state in a mobile-first, read-optimized layout.
  • FR-252 — Project/portfolio browsing. Browse projects and portfolios with search, filter, and detail views.
  • FR-253 — Run/stage views. View run and stage state, telemetry, and logs (summarized for mobile).
  • FR-254 — HIL gate views. View pending HIL gates and their context.
  • FR-255 — Failure views. View failures with diagnosis and links to detail.
  • FR-256 — Auto-mode decision views. View auto-mode decisions and escalations from the F-7 ledger.
  • FR-257 — Multi-project run group views. View run-group aggregate and per-child state.
  • FR-258 — Notification-driven alerts. Push notifications for gates, failures, escalations, and completions.
  • FR-259 — Command delegation to F-6. Route every command action to the F-6 surface for preview, confirmation, and audit; never execute directly.
  • FR-260 — Offline read-only last-known state. Show the last-known state read-only when offline.
  • FR-261 — Connectivity degradation. Indicate connectivity/offline status and recover cleanly on reconnect.
  • FR-262 — Mobile security. Support device lock, session timeout, and secure token storage.
  • FR-263 — Mobile privacy. Prevent sensitive data leakage via notifications and screenshots where configured.
  • FR-264 — Mobile accessibility. Meet touch-target, contrast, and screen-reader requirements.
  • FR-265 — Notification preferences. Let operators configure notification categories and quiet hours.
  • FR-266 — Install behaviour. Support add-to-home-screen / PWA-style install and safe updates.
  • FR-267 — Mobile observability. Expose crash, latency, and connectivity metrics.

F-12: Test Management

  • FR-276 — Test case registry. Register, read, list, update, and retire test cases with owner, tags, and severity.
  • FR-277 — Versioned test definitions. Version test definitions immutably; existing bindings pin to a version.
  • FR-278 — Suite composition. Compose test cases into suites.
  • FR-279 — Test plan composition. Compose suites/cases into test plans with sequencing and selection rules.
  • FR-280 — Plan binding. Bind plans to pipeline stages and projects so a stage can consume a governed quality gate.
  • FR-281 — Test execution. Execute tests on demand, on schedule, or as a governed stage of a pipeline run (driven by F-3).
  • FR-282 — Result capture and evidence. Capture deterministic outcomes and attach evidence (logs, artifacts, diffs).
  • FR-283 — Quality gate evaluation. Evaluate plan-level quality gates and publish pass/fail verdicts that stage advancement consumes.
  • FR-284 — Coverage tracking. Track coverage over pipeline artifacts and report gaps.
  • FR-285 — Regression and flakiness management. Detect regressions and flaky tests and manage them (quarantine, re-run).
  • FR-286 — Waiver/approval. Allow authorized waivers of failing gates with rationale and expiry.
  • FR-287 — Reporting. Provide test reports (pass rate, trends, coverage, gate history).
  • FR-288 — Audit trail. Retain an immutable audit trail of test definitions, runs, results, and gate verdicts.
  • FR-289 — Definition API. Publish a stable API of test case/suite/plan definitions.
  • FR-290 — Run API. Publish a stable API to start and query test runs.
  • FR-291 — Result/gate verdict API. Publish a stable API of results and gate verdicts for consumers.
  • FR-292 — Scheduling. Schedule recurring test plans with run-window and concurrency rules.
  • FR-293 — Test authorization. Enforce who may define, run, waive, and view tests.

F-13: API-First Access

  • FR-301 — Versioning and lifecycle. Version the API surface and manage introduce/deprecate/retire lifecycle.
  • FR-302 — Contract publication. Publish machine-readable, contract-verified API specifications.
  • FR-303 — Client authentication. Authenticate API clients via supported credential types.
  • FR-304 — Scope-based authorization. Enforce per-client scopes mirroring dashboard capabilities.
  • FR-305 — Credential lifecycle. Create, rotate, revoke, and expire client credentials.
  • FR-306 — Uniform envelope. Apply a consistent request/response envelope across all operations.
  • FR-307 — Error model. Provide a uniform, machine-readable error model with stable codes.
  • FR-308 — Pagination/filtering/fieldsets. Support pagination, filtering, sorting, and sparse fieldsets uniformly.
  • FR-309 — Idempotency. Support idempotency keys on mutating operations.
  • FR-310 — Concurrency control. Support optimistic concurrency via ETags/revisions.
  • FR-311 — Async long-running operations. Model long-running work as pollable operations with status and results.
  • FR-312 — Streaming telemetry. Provide a streaming surface for live run telemetry.
  • FR-313 — Webhook/event subscriptions. Allow clients to subscribe to events via webhooks with retries and signing.
  • FR-314 — Bulk operations. Provide bulk endpoints with per-item results.
  • FR-315 — Dry-run preview. Provide dry-run/preview for supported mutating operations.
  • FR-316 — Rate limiting and quotas. Enforce per-client rate limits and quotas with clear signalling.
  • FR-317 — Capability discovery. Expose a discovery endpoint describing capabilities and versions.
  • FR-318 — Activity auditing. Audit all API activity (actor, operation, outcome).
  • FR-319 — SDK/CLI generation. Generate SDKs and a CLI from the published contract.
  • FR-320 — Sandbox environment. Provide an isolated sandbox for safe client development.

F-14: API Reuse and Extension Analysis

  • FR-326 — Contract inventory snapshot. Continuously snapshot the versioned API contracts published by F-13.
  • FR-327 — Demand registry. Maintain a registry of registered consumer demand for capabilities.
  • FR-328 — Matching and gap detection. Match demand against contracts and detect gaps.
  • FR-329 — Reuse/extension classification. Classify each proposed capability as reuse, additive extension, versioned change, or new.
  • FR-330 — Duplication/overlap findings. Detect duplicate or overlapping operations across contracts.
  • FR-331 — Coverage scoring. Compute per-area coverage scores.
  • FR-332 — Review/decision record. Record review outcomes and decisions for each proposed capability.
  • FR-333 — Published reuse guidance. Publish guidance recommending reuse before new introduction.
  • FR-334 — Read-only guarantee. Never mutate, deprecate, or publish contracts on another feature's behalf.
  • FR-335 — Refresh/scheduling. Refresh analysis on contract changes or a schedule.
  • FR-336 — Reporting. Provide analysis reports (gaps, overlaps, coverage).
  • FR-337 — Authorization. Enforce who may view or contribute demand records.
  • FR-338 — Audit. Audit analysis runs and decisions.
  • FR-339 — Analysis API. Publish a stable API for snapshots, findings, and guidance.

F-15: Deployment Options

  • FR-351 — Deployment-target catalog. Define supported deployment targets (e.g., managed cloud, self-hosted, air-gapped) and their guarantees.
  • FR-352 — Topology/sizing profiles. Provide selectable topology and sizing profiles.
  • FR-353 — Provisioning/bootstrap. Provide a provisioning/bootstrap path per target.
  • FR-354 — Config and secret management. Define a configuration and secret-management contract per target.
  • FR-355 — Upgrade/rollback. Support versioned upgrade with safe rollback.
  • FR-356 — Scaling. Support horizontal/vertical scaling per profile.
  • FR-357 — High availability. Provide HA configurations with defined failure domains.
  • FR-358 — Backup/restore. Provide backup and point-in-time restore.
  • FR-359 — Observability wiring. Wire metrics, logs, and tracing per target.
  • FR-360 — Network/TLS and identity wiring. Configure network, TLS, and identity integration per target.
  • FR-361 — Data residency. Enforce data-residency placement options.
  • FR-362 — Air-gapped/offline delivery. Support fully offline delivery and updates.
  • FR-363 — Entitlement binding. Bind entitlements/licenses to installations.
  • FR-364 — Drift detection. Detect drift between declared and running configuration.
  • FR-365 — Multi-environment promotion. Support promotion across environments.
  • FR-366 — Hardening profiles. Provide hardening profiles (CIS-style baselines).
  • FR-367 — Cost estimation. Estimate cost per profile.
  • FR-368 — Decommissioning. Support safe decommissioning and data disposal.
  • FR-369 — Deployment manifest/API. Publish a machine-readable manifest describing a running installation and a stable deployment API.
  • FR-370 — Health/parity reports. Publish health and cross-target parity reports.

F-16: Voice Reference Alignment

  • FR-376 — Reference voice registry. Register and manage reference voices and their locale.
  • FR-377 — Sample ingestion. Ingest source audio samples for a reference.
  • FR-378 — Preprocessing/normalization. Preprocess and normalize samples (level, denoise, format).
  • FR-379 — Phonetic/transcript alignment. Align phonetics and transcripts to the samples.
  • FR-380 — Speaker-embedding extraction. Extract speaker embeddings per sample.
  • FR-381 — Alignment scoring and verdicts. Compute objective alignment scores and pass/review/fail verdicts.
  • FR-382 — Immutable profile versioning. Compose immutable reference-profile versions.
  • FR-383 — Approval/retirement workflow. Approve and retire profiles with role gating.
  • FR-384 — Binding to languages/locales. Bind profiles to languages/locales and the Voice Companion's resolution rules.
  • FR-385 — Drift monitoring. Monitor profile drift against fresh samples.
  • FR-386 — Cross-sample consistency. Enforce cross-sample consistency checks.
  • FR-387 — Search and import/export. Search references and import/export profiles.
  • FR-388 — Role-gated access. Restrict alignment operations to authorized roles.
  • FR-389 — Immutable audit trail. Retain an immutable audit trail of alignments and decisions.
  • FR-390 — Alignment settings/presets. Provide alignment settings and reusable presets.
  • FR-391 — Batch alignment. Run alignment in batches with per-item results.
  • FR-392 — Failure diagnosis. Diagnose alignment failures with actionable causes.
  • FR-393 — Preview synthesis. Preview-synthesize from a candidate profile.
  • FR-394 — Completion notifications. Notify on alignment completion and verdicts.
  • FR-395 — Reference API. Publish a stable API of reference profiles, alignments, and verdicts for F-10 to consume.

Non-Functional Requirements

Cross-cutting quality attributes for Product Forge. Each NFR is scoped to exactly one feature (the feature that owns the corresponding functional behaviour) and is numbered within that feature's allocated range. Acceptance of a feature requires demonstrating its NFRs in addition to its functional requirements.

F-1: Central Pipeline Control Plane

  • NFR-1 (Performance): Project/run registry reads (list, detail) p95 < 300 ms and p99 < 800 ms at rated load (10,000 projects, 100,000 runs in history).
  • NFR-2 (Performance): Control commands (start / pause / resume / cancel / retry) are acknowledged p95 < 500 ms; acknowledgement is returned only after the command is durably persisted.
  • NFR-3 (Scalability): A single installation supports ≥ 10,000 active projects, ≥ 100,000 historical runs, and ≥ 2,000 pipeline-definition versions without breaching NFR-1/NFR-2; read paths scale horizontally.
  • NFR-4 (Availability): Control-plane API availability ≥ 99.9% monthly. If the execution subsystem is unavailable, the registry continues serving reads and queues commands, clearly marked as pending.
  • NFR-5 (Data integrity): Registry writes are durable (committed before acknowledgement) and concurrency-safe via optimistic version checks (configuration revision). No lost updates on concurrent edits.
  • NFR-6 (Security): Lifecycle operations and run/stage control commands are authenticated and authorized per project scope via RBAC; destructive operations (permanent delete) require an elevated role.
  • NFR-7 (Auditability): Every lifecycle and control command produces an immutable audit record (actor, target, timestamp, before/after, outcome) retained ≥ 400 days and tamper-evident.
  • NFR-8 (Data residency): Project, pipeline and run metadata are stored in the configured residency region; no cross-region replication occurs without explicit opt-in configuration.
  • NFR-9 (Deployment/environment): The control plane behaves identically across cloud, self-hosted and air-gapped profiles; core registry and orchestration have no mandatory external network dependency.
  • NFR-10 (Observability): Registry and orchestration expose metrics, distributed traces, structured logs and a health/readiness endpoint; stage transition and gate backlog metrics are emitted per run.
  • NFR-11 (Backup/restore): Registry backup RPO ≤ 15 minutes; documented restore RTO ≤ 4 hours; restore is verified by automated drill.
  • NFR-12 (Idempotency): All control commands accept an idempotency key; replay of the same key returns the original outcome without re-dispatching work.
  • NFR-13 (Concurrency): ≥ 200 concurrent run-control commands are processed without stage double-dispatch or lost commands; stage dispatch is exactly-once effective (at-least-once delivery + idempotent handlers).
  • NFR-14 (Retention/lifecycle): Run history retention is configurable, defaults to 400 days; deletion follows a documented soft-delete → hard-delete policy that never mutates historical audit records.
  • NFR-15 (API lifecycle): The registry API is versioned; breaking changes require a new major version with a deprecation window of ≥ 2 minor releases and documented migration guidance.

F-2: Project Creation and Model Tier Management

  • NFR-16 (Performance): Wizard step save p95 < 400 ms; model-tier resolution p95 < 200 ms when cached, < 800 ms cold.
  • NFR-17 (Performance): End-to-end project creation p95 < 2 s excluding asynchronous pipeline materialization.
  • NFR-18 (Scalability): Supports ≥ 500 concurrent in-progress creation drafts and ≥ 200 predefined + ≥ 500 custom tier definitions per installation.
  • NFR-19 (Availability): Creation is available whenever the registry (F-1) is available; drafts are persisted so an interrupted creation can be resumed after restart or reconnection.
  • NFR-20 (Security): Tier creation/editing is restricted to authorized roles and audited; provider credentials are referenced by identifier and never stored or transmitted in plaintext within tier definitions.
  • NFR-21 (Security): Tier validation enforces provider/model allowlists and prevents a tier from granting more capability (autonomy, budget, data scope) than the creating role holds.
  • NFR-22 (Data residency): Tier definitions and provider bindings are stored under the installation's residency policy; credential material never leaves the residency region.
  • NFR-23 (Deployment/environment): Creation and tier management operate air-gapped, with the tier catalog seedable from a signed offline bundle and locally hosted model providers supported.
  • NFR-24 (Accessibility/usability): The creation wizard is fully keyboard navigable, screen-reader labelled and WCAG 2.1 AA compliant; field validation feedback is announced.
  • NFR-25 (Data integrity): A custom tier definition is immutable once bound to a project; subsequent edits produce a new version, and projects retain a reference to the version they were created with.
  • NFR-26 (Performance, validation): Inline field validation responds < 100 ms and does not block typing.
  • NFR-27 (Portability): Tier definitions are exportable/importable as a documented machine-readable manifest, enabling promotion between environments.

F-3: E2E Pipeline Initiation and Monitoring

  • NFR-31 (Performance): Run initiation returns a stable run identifier p95 < 500 ms, before any stage dispatch begins.
  • NFR-32 (Performance): Live telemetry is delivered end-to-end with p95 < 2 s latency; dashboard state is at most 5 s stale during normal operation.
  • NFR-33 (Throughput): Supports ≥ 100 concurrent runs and ≥ 20 sustained stage transitions per second per installation.
  • NFR-34 (Scalability): Handles ≥ 10,000 runs/day across the registered 32-stage model with complete ordered event history retained per run.
  • NFR-35 (Availability): Execution engine availability ≥ 99.5%; in-flight runs resume after a restart with no loss of committed stage state (durable checkpointing).
  • NFR-36 (Correctness): Stage dispatch is exactly-once effective; no duplicate external side effects on retry, restart or replay.
  • NFR-37 (Reliability): Transient stage failures are retried per declared policy; exhausted retries fail closed with an actionable, itemized reason and never silently advance the run.
  • NFR-38 (Security): Run/stage commands are authorized per project scope; telemetry, artifacts and logs are filtered by role so an actor sees only what they may read.
  • NFR-39 (Auditability): Each run has an immutable, ordered event log recording every transition, input/output hash, actor and decision; the log is not mutable after write.
  • NFR-40 (Data residency): Run telemetry, stage artifacts and logs are stored in the configured residency region.
  • NFR-41 (Observability): Per-run traces, per-stage metrics, gate backlog depth, failure rates and queue latency are emitted; threshold breaches raise alerts.
  • NFR-42 (Recovery): After an unexpected engine termination, run progress converges back to the next stage transition within ≤ 60 s.
  • NFR-43 (Resource governance): Per-run CPU, memory, token, time and cost budgets are enforced; runaway runs are halted and reported rather than allowed to consume unbounded resources.
  • NFR-44 (Deployment/environment): Runs execute air-gapped with no mandatory telemetry egress; all runtime dependencies are packaged with the installation.
  • NFR-45 (Retention): Run telemetry and artifact retention is configurable, defaults to 400 days, and respects the residency policy on expiry and destruction.

F-4: Portfolio Management

  • NFR-46 (Performance): Portfolio roll-up read p95 < 800 ms for a portfolio of 100 member projects.
  • NFR-47 (Performance): Background roll-up cache refresh completes within ≤ 30 s of a member-state change; explicit operator-triggered refresh returns p95 < 2 s.
  • NFR-48 (Scalability): Supports ≥ 1,000 portfolios and ≥ 5,000 projects per portfolio without breaching NFR-46.
  • NFR-49 (Availability): When the roll-up pipeline is degraded, the portfolio view serves last-known-good data with an explicit staleness indicator instead of failing or showing misleading zeros.
  • NFR-50 (Consistency): Roll-ups are eventually consistent with a bounded lag ≤ 30 s and are always displayed with an "as of" timestamp.
  • NFR-51 (Security): Portfolio membership changes and scoped bulk operations are restricted to portfolio owner/admin roles and enforced per affected project.
  • NFR-52 (Security): Roll-ups apply row-level filtering so an aggregate never reveals the existence or state of a project the viewer is not authorized to read.
  • NFR-53 (Auditability): Membership changes, bulk operations and roll-up-affecting configuration changes produce immutable audit records.
  • NFR-54 (Data residency): Portfolio metadata and derived roll-ups are stored in the residency region; no aggregation crosses region boundaries.
  • NFR-55 (Deployment/environment): Portfolio management works in cloud, self-hosted and air-gapped profiles with equivalent semantics.
  • NFR-56 (Safety): Bulk operations are bounded (maximum target count per operation), previewable before execution, and cancellable while in progress.
  • NFR-57 (Reporting): Roll-up export for 5,000 projects completes ≤ 5 s and is available in documented CSV and JSON formats.

F-5: Multi-Project Runs

  • NFR-61 (Performance): Multi-project run group creation p95 < 1 s; child run fan-out begins within ≤ 5 s of group start.
  • NFR-62 (Scalability): Supports ≥ 500 child runs per group and ≥ 20 concurrently executing groups per installation.
  • NFR-63 (Availability): A group tolerates partial child failure and continues according to its declared failure policy; group state remains queryable throughout.
  • NFR-64 (Consistency): Parent group status is derived deterministically from child states by a documented rule; no phantom or undefined aggregate states are observable.
  • NFR-65 (Idempotency): Group creation and child fan-out are idempotent; restart or replay never produces duplicate child runs.
  • NFR-66 (Resource fairness): Group dispatch respects global concurrency limits and cannot starve single-project runs; a documented fairness policy is enforced.
  • NFR-67 (Security): A group's project scope is authorized at creation and re-validated at dispatch; an actor cannot include projects they cannot command.
  • NFR-68 (Auditability): Parent-child lineage, shared/overridden parameters and per-child outcomes produce an immutable audit trail.
  • NFR-69 (Data residency): Group metadata, aggregation and child telemetry are stored in the residency region.
  • NFR-70 (Deployment/environment): Multi-project runs operate identically in air-gapped deployments with no external coordination service required.
  • NFR-71 (Observability): Aggregated group progress, per-child drill-down, fan-out latency and reconciliation events are exposed as metrics and traces.
  • NFR-72 (Recovery): After a crash, group reconciliation converges to a consistent parent/child state within ≤ 2 minutes without duplicating child work.

F-6: Manual Input and Operation Control

  • NFR-76 (Performance): Manual command acknowledgement p95 < 300 ms; dry-run/preview p95 < 1 s.
  • NFR-77 (Scalability): ≥ 100 concurrent operators can issue commands without visible contention or lost commands.
  • NFR-78 (Availability): The command surface is available whenever the control plane is; when execution is unavailable, commands are queued with an accurate pending state rather than rejected silently.
  • NFR-79 (Concurrency/conflict): Concurrent conflicting commands are resolved by optimistic version check and return a deterministic 409-style conflict with the current state, never a silent last-write-wins.
  • NFR-80 (Security): Every action is role-gated; destructive and system-wide actions require an elevated role plus explicit confirmation.
  • NFR-81 (Security): Emergency system-wide halt is restricted to authorized roles with an optionally configurable two-person rule.
  • NFR-82 (Auditability): Every manual command (including dry-runs and rejected commands) is immutably audited with actor, target, parameters, before/after state and result.
  • NFR-83 (Safety): Preview/dry-run never mutates authoritative state or emits externally observable side effects.
  • NFR-84 (Data residency): Command audit records, including operator input payloads, are stored in the residency region with no cross-border egress.
  • NFR-85 (Deployment/environment): The manual command surface is available in all deployment profiles, including air-gapped, with no external dependency.
  • NFR-86 (Reliability): Commands are atomic — a failed command leaves the target run/stage in its prior well-defined state and never in a partial state.
  • NFR-87 (Retention): Command audit records are retained ≥ 400 days and are not alterable by any later command.

F-7: Auto Mode

  • NFR-91 (Performance): Policy evaluation per autonomous decision p95 < 200 ms; automated HIL gate resolution completes within ≤ 5 s of gate availability.
  • NFR-92 (Scalability): ≥ 10,000 automated decisions per day and ≥ 200 concurrently auto-managed runs per installation.
  • NFR-93 (Availability/safety): Auto Mode fails closed: loss or unavailability of the policy evaluator escalates to a human rather than auto-approving or auto-advancing.
  • NFR-94 (Determinism): Identical inputs plus the same recorded policy version yield the same decision; the policy version used is recorded on every decision.
  • NFR-95 (Security): Changing autonomy level or policy requires an elevated role and is audited; the kill switch is always reachable by authorized operators and reverts to manual control immediately.
  • NFR-96 (Safety): Budget, time and risk guardrails are enforced by the platform and cannot be widened by a policy; a policy attempting to exceed them is rejected on save.
  • NFR-97 (Auditability/explainability): Every decision is recorded in an immutable ledger citing the exact rule, inputs and outcome, sufficient for a human to reconstruct the reasoning.
  • NFR-98 (Data residency): The decision ledger and policy definitions are stored in the residency region.
  • NFR-99 (Deployment/environment): Auto Mode evaluates policies locally without external services and operates in air-gapped deployments.
  • NFR-100 (Observability): Decision volume, escalation rate, guardrail trips and kill-switch activations are emitted as metrics with alerting.
  • NFR-101 (Recovery): Auto Mode resumes decision-making after a restart without duplicating previously applied actions (idempotent decisions).

F-8: Dashboard UX Component System

  • NFR-106 (Performance UX): First Contentful Paint < 1 s, Largest Contentful Paint < 2.5 s, Time to Interactive < 3 s on a representative 3G-class connection; page load < 2 s on broadband.
  • NFR-107 (Performance UX): Interaction (input/click) response < 100 ms; Cumulative Layout Shift < 0.1 across all standard surfaces.
  • NFR-108 (Scalability): Tables/lists render 10,000 rows via virtualization without frame drops; design-token or theme changes apply without full page reload.
  • NFR-109 (Accessibility): WCAG 2.1 AA across all composed surfaces — text contrast ≥ 4.5:1, UI contrast ≥ 3:1, complete keyboard navigation, semantic HTML with ARIA where needed, visible 2 px focus indicators, skip-to-content link, no flashing > 3 Hz.
  • NFR-110 (Availability): The application shell is cacheable and degrades to read-only with a persistent offline banner rather than an error page.
  • NFR-111 (Compatibility): Supported on the latest two versions of Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Safari; responsive from 320 px to 4K.
  • NFR-112 (Security): No secrets in client bundles; strict Content Security Policy; all domain-sourced content is output-encoded to prevent XSS.
  • NFR-113 (Internationalization): Locale selector in settings; dates, times, numbers and currency formatted per user locale; layout is RTL-ready.
  • NFR-114 (Theming): Light, dark and high-contrast themes, persisted per user and applied without flash-of-wrong-theme.
  • NFR-115 (Real-time UX): Streaming updates do not cause layout thrash; optimistic UI reconciles to authoritative state and visibly reverts on rejection.
