API-2 — Component contract registry

GET /ux/components → machine-readable manifest of primitives, composed components, feature components: name, version, props/variants, required ARIA roles, keyboard interactions, and the domain features they render. GET /ux/components/{componentName} → single contract with props, slots, and accessibility guarantees. Rules: a component may render only state published by the authoritative feature; no component contract may define or mutate domain state.

API-3 — User interface preferences

Method Path Purpose
GET /ux/preferences Read caller's theme, density, locale, motion, notification prefs
PUT /ux/preferences Replace preferences (validated, server-persisted)

Rules: unknown enum values → 422; reducedMotion and highContrast are honored on every surface.

API-4 — Real-time update contract

GET /ux/stream-contract → declarative description of every event channel the dashboard subscribes to, its payload schema, and the components that consume it (the streams themselves are owned by F-3, F-5, F-6, F-7). Rules: the contract is versioned; a breaking payload change requires a new major contract version.

API-5 — Contextual help and empty/error copy registry

GET /ux/copy?surface=<surface>&state=<empty|loading|error|offline> → canonical copy strings with placement (inline, toast, banner, page) and an associated action. GET /ux/help/tooltips?component={name} → tooltip and ?-icon help content.

Rules: every list/page surface must have registered empty, loading, and error copy entries; an unregistered state returns 404 not_found and fails the contract check.

API-6 — Internationalization resources

GET /ux/i18n/{locale} → message catalog, plural rules, date/number/currency formats, text direction (ltr|rtl). GET /ux/i18n → supported locales and coverage percentages. Rules: missing keys fall back to the default locale and are reported in meta.missingKeys[] for translation follow-up.

F-9: Global AI Chat Companion

API-1 — Companion session

Method Path Purpose
POST /assistant/sessions Open session bound to a context
GET /assistant/sessions/{sessionId} Read session and binding
DELETE /assistant/sessions/{sessionId} End session

Create request { "context": { "kind": "project|run|stage|gate|portfolio|run_group|auto_mode", "id": "run_9f2" } } Rules: context is required; the session stores the binding at open time and surfaces contextStale: true if the bound object changes materially.

API-2 — Messages and grounded answers

POST /assistant/sessions/{sessionId}/messages → { "text": "string", "stream": true } Response 202 with text/event-stream when stream: true, else a single completed message. Stream events: message.delta, citation, tool.read, message.completed, message.refused, error.

event: citation
data: {"citationId":"cit_3","objectKind":"run","objectId":"run_9f2",
       "field":"status","observedAt":"ISO-8601","surface":"F-3 API-5"}

Rules: every factual claim about pipeline state must be accompanied by ≥ 1 citation; if grounding fails, the companion emits message.refused with { "reason": "insufficient_grounding" } rather than an unsourced answer; the companion never executes a command — it may only draft one (API-4).

API-3 — Conversation history

GET /assistant/sessions/{sessionId}/messages?cursor= → ordered turns with citations preserved; read-only, append-only. GET /assistant/sessions?cursor= → the caller's sessions (never another user's without an explicit delegated scope).

API-4 — Context resolution and command drafting

GET /assistant/context?kind=run&id=run_9f2 → resolved context summary built only from authoritative reads, with per-field source attribution. POST /assistant/sessions/{sessionId}/draft-commands → returns an F-6 command draft, not an executed command:

{ "draftId": "drf_1", "command": { "target": {"kind":"run","id":"run_9f2"},
  "action": "retry", "arguments": { "fromStageId": "stg_3" } },
  "previewUrl": "/api/v1/commands:preview", "requiresConfirmation": true }

Rules: the draft is inert until submitted to F-6; drafts are never auto-submitted; scopes the operator lacks cause 403 auth.forbidden at draft time and again at execution time.

API-5 — Citation resolution

GET /assistant/citations/{citationId} → the authoritative snapshot the citation pointed at, including surface, objectId, observedAt, and a staleness assessment.

API-6 — Degraded deterministic help

GET /assistant/help/search?q= → deterministic help-center and docs results with no AI dependency. Rules: when the AI backend is unavailable the companion responds 503 dependency.unavailable for AI turns and automatically offers results from this endpoint; the UI must present the degraded mode without impedance.

API-7 — Feedback

POST /assistant/messages/{messageId}/feedback → { "verdict": "helpful|not_helpful", "comment": "string|null" }. Append-only; used to evaluate grounding quality, never to mutate the message.

F-10: Voice Companion

API-1 — Voice session and microphone lifecycle

Method Path Purpose
POST /voice/sessions Open voice session (bind to an F-9 session + context)
POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}:activate Begin listening (explicit user gesture required)
POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}:deactivate Stop listening / release microphone
DELETE /voice/sessions/{sessionId} End session and discard ephemeral audio
GET /voice/sessions/{sessionId} Session state (idle|listening|thinking|speaking|error)

Create request { "assistantSessionId": "as_1", "locale": "en-US", "retainAudio": false } Rules: retainAudio defaults to false; the server never stores raw audio unless the operator explicitly opts in; state transitions are serialized — a second activate while speaking triggers barge-in (API-4) rather than erroring.

API-2 — Streaming speech-to-text

POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/audio (Content-Type: audio/pcm;rate=16000, chunked or audio/wav full utterance) Responses: text/event-stream with transcript.partial, transcript.final, endpoint.detected. Rules: a final transcript is required before F-9 is invoked; empty or unintelligible input yields transcript.final with confidence < 0.4 and triggers a spoken clarification prompt instead of a guess.

API-3 — Spoken synthesis of grounded answers

POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/speak → { "messageId": "msg_9", "format": "opus|mp3|pcm", "voiceProfileId": "vrp_3" } Response 200 audio/* streaming, or text/event-stream with speech.start, speech.chunk, speech.citation, speech.end. Rules: the spoken text is the grounded F-9 answer verbatim in substance; citations are marked by speech.citation events at the point they occur in the utterance so the client can render them visually while speaking; the voice profile must resolve through F-16.

API-4 — Barge-in

POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}:barge-in → { "atChunk": 12 } Rules: synthesis stops within 300 ms; the interrupted message is marked interrupted=true; attempts to barge in while idle return 409 conflict.invalid_state.

API-5 — Hands-free gate announcements and decisions

GET /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/announcements (text/event-stream) → gate.opened, run.failed, auto_mode.escalation announcements, each carrying the F-3/F-7 object ids. POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/announcements/{announcementId}:acknowledge → spoken acknowledgement recorded. Rules: voice may only draft a gate decision or command (handed to F-9 API-4 and then F-6); a spoken "approve" produces a confirmation prompt and never an immediate execution.

API-6 — Voice navigation

POST /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/navigate → { "utterance": "open run 9f2" } → { "resolved": { "kind": "run", "id": "run_9f2" }, "confidence": 0.91 } Rules: navigation resolves within the caller's authorization scope; low-confidence or ambiguous targets (confidence < 0.7 or > 1 match) return 422 with candidates[] for spoken disambiguation.

API-7 — Transcripts, privacy, and settings

Method Path Purpose
GET /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/transcript Transcript turns (retained only if opted in)
DELETE /voice/sessions/{sessionId}/audio Purge any retained audio
GET /voice/settings Locale, voice profile binding, retention, wake behaviour
PUT /voice/settings Update settings

Rules: purge is immediate and irreversible; retention settings are per-user, not per-device.

F-11: Mobile Dashboard Companion

API-1 — Mobile bootstrap (aggregated read model)

GET /mobile/bootstrap?since=<ISO-8601> → one round trip returning: caller identity/scopes, projects (paged), portfolios, active runs with current stage, open HIL gates, recent failures, auto-mode escalations, run groups, plus serverTime and snapshotVersion. Rules: read-only; the payload is composed exclusively of authoritative F-1…F-7 data; snapshotVersion is required for the next delta call.

API-2 — Mobile views

GET /mobile/views/{view}?cursor= where view ∈ projects | runs | run_detail | gates | portfolios | escalations | auto_mode Rules: each view mirrors desktop semantics with a reduced field set; no mobile-only state exists; every field is traceable to its owning feature's API.

API-3 — Offline snapshot and delta sync

GET /mobile/offline-snapshot → last-known state bundle with snapshotVersion, generatedAt, and an explicit staleAfter (default 15 minutes). GET /mobile/bootstrap?since=<snapshotVersion> → delta of changes. Rules: offline snapshots are stamped readOnly: true; the client must display an offline badge and must not offer any write affordance while disconnected.

API-4 — Push notification registration

Method Path Purpose
POST /mobile/devices Register device token { "platform": "ios|android", "token": "string", "locale": "en-US" }
DELETE /mobile/devices/{deviceId} Unregister
GET /mobile/notifications In-app notification center (paged)
POST /mobile/notifications:read Mark read { "notificationIds": ["n_1"] }

Rules: notifications carry only object references and safe summaries — no secrets, no parameters, no rationale text; tapping a notification deep-links to the corresponding view; push is opt-in per category.

API-5 — Mobile command delegation

POST /mobile/commands:draft → forwards to F-9 API-4 and returns an F-6 draft; the mobile client then calls F-6 API-2 preview and F-6 API-1 execute. Rules: the mobile companion exposes no direct mutation endpoint of its own; every write path is F-6; 403 from F-6 is surfaced verbatim.

API-6 — Device-bound session and security

POST /mobile/sessions → device-bound session { "deviceId", "biometricAssertion": "string|null" } → short-lived access token + refresh token. POST /mobile/sessions:refresh → rotate. DELETE /mobile/sessions/{sessionId} → remote revoke. Rules: tokens are device-bound and revocable per device; screenshots/parameters can be suppressed per project policy; a revoked device receives 401 on its next call and the client must wipe cached state.

API-7 — Capability and degradation reporting

GET /mobile/capabilities → feature availability flags (AI available, voice available, streaming available, notifications available) + degraded: true with the affected surfaces listed. Rules: the client must render degraded surfaces as read-only with an explanatory banner rather than hiding them silently.

F-12: Test Management

API-1 — Test case registry and versioning

Method Path Purpose
GET /test-cases List (filter[projectId], filter[tag], q)
POST /test-cases Create
GET /test-cases/{testCaseId} Read with current version
PATCH /test-cases/{testCaseId} Create a new draft version
POST /test-cases/{testCaseId}/versions Publish immutable version
GET /test-cases/{testCaseId}/versions List versions

Version body { "assertions": [ {"kind": "equality|schema|regex|threshold|semantic", "target": "...", "expected": ...} ], "inputs": {}, "tags": [], "determinism": "deterministic|nondeterministic" } Rules: published versions are immutable (405 on edit); a published case may be quarantined but not deleted if referenced by a plan; determinism: nondeterministic requires an explicit flake policy at plan binding time.

API-2 — Suites

GET|POST /test-suites, GET|PATCH|DELETE /test-suites/{suiteId}, POST /test-suites/{suiteId}/members ({ "testCaseVersionIds": [] }), DELETE /test-suites/{suiteId}/members/{testCaseVersionId}. Rules: a suite references versions, never floating case ids; deleting a member only affects future suite runs.

API-3 — Plans and stage/project binding

GET|POST /test-plans, GET|PATCH /test-plans/{planId}, POST /test-plans/{planId}/versions, POST /test-plans/{planId}:bind Bind request { "projectId": "prj_1", "stageId": "stg_6", "trigger": "stage_entry|stage_exit", "requiredForAdvancement": true, "gatePolicy": { "maxFailures": 0, "allowWaivers": true } } Rules: a binding with requiredForAdvancement: true makes the plan's gate verdict authoritative for F-3 stage advancement; a stage may have at most one required plan, otherwise 409 conflict.invalid_state.

API-4 — Test execution

Method Path Purpose
POST /test-runs Start on demand { "planVersionId", "projectId", "scope": {...} }
GET /test-runs List (filter[projectId], filter[planId], filter[status])
GET /test-runs/{testRunId} Read with counters
POST /test-runs/{testRunId}:cancel Cancel
POST /test-runs/{testRunId}:retry Retry failed cases only

Rules: pipeline-triggered runs are created by F-3 as a governed stage step and carry the originating runId/stageId; results are deterministic per case version + inputs; a retry never rewrites the earlier run — it creates a new one linked by parentTestRunId.

API-5 — Results and evidence

GET /test-runs/{testRunId}/results?cursor= → per-case { testCaseVersionId, status, durationMs, assertions[] (actual vs expected), failureSignature }. GET /test-results/{resultId}/evidence → structured artifacts (logs, diffs, response bodies) with stable references and hashes. Rules: a failed result must carry at least one failing assertion with actual and expected values; evidence is content-addressed and immutable.

API-6 — Quality gate verdicts

GET /quality-gates/{gateId} → verdict derived from the bound plan run. POST /quality-gates/{gateId}:evaluate → recompute (idempotent within the same evidence revision). Response { "verdict": "pass|fail|waived", "planVersionId", "testRunId", "blockingFailures": ["tc_1"], "waivers": [], "evaluatedAt": "ISO-8601", "evidenceRevision": "evr_9" } Rules: the verdict is the only input F-3 may consume for governed advancement; recomputation against a different evidence revision produces a new immutable verdict record superseding the old one.

API-7 — Coverage tracking

GET /coverage?projectId=&artifactKind=&baseline= → coverage percentage per pipeline artifact, uncovered artifacts list, and delta vs. baseline. Rules: coverage computation is derived from authoritative result evidence; unavailable artifact inventory degrades to partial: true rather than erroring.

API-8 — Regression and flakiness

GET /regressions?projectId=&since= → cases that passed in a baseline and now fail, with first-seen timestamps. GET /test-cases/{testCaseId}/flakiness → pass/fail history, flake rate, signature clusters. POST /test-cases/{testCaseId}:quarantine → { "reason": "string", "expiresAt": "ISO-8601|null" } POST /test-cases/{testCaseId}:unquarantine Rules: quarantined cases are excluded from gate verdicts but still executed and reported as quarantined; quarantine always expires or is explicitly reviewed — indefinite quarantine is not representable.

API-9 — Waivers and approval

POST /test-waivers → { "testCaseVersionId": "tcv_1", "planVersionId": "tpv_2", "reason": "string (>=20 chars)", "expiresAt": "ISO-8601", "approvedByUserId": "usr_7" } GET /test-waivers?filter[planId]= → active, expired, and revoked waivers. Rules: waivers require the test:waive scope and a human approver (never an Auto Mode decision); waivers are time-boxed and appear in the gate verdict's waivers[].

API-10 — Reporting and audit

GET /test-reports?projectId=&from=&to=&format= → aggregated pass rates, top failure signatures, gate outcomes, coverage trend. POST /test-reports:export → 202 + artifact reference. GET /test-audit?filter[objectKind]=&filter[objectId]= → immutable trail of case versions, plan bindings, runs, verdicts, waivers, and quarantines.

F-13: API-First Access

API-1 — Versioning and contract publication

GET /api/versions → supported versions with status: current|supported|deprecated|sunset, deprecationDate, sunsetDate, and links to each contract. GET /api/v1/openapi.json → machine-readable OpenAPI document for the version (also ?format=yaml). GET /api/v1/changelog → additive vs. breaking changes per release. Rules: breaking changes require a new major version; a deprecated version advertises Deprecation and Sunset response headers on every call; contract documents are generated from the running implementation, never hand-maintained separately.

API-2 — Client authentication and scope authorization

POST /oauth/token (authorization-code + PKCE, client-credentials, refresh-token grants) → { "access_token", "token_type", "expires_in", "refresh_token?", "scope" } POST /oauth/revoke → revoke a token or refresh token. GET|POST /api-keys, GET /api-keys/{keyId}, POST /api-keys/{keyId}:rotate, DELETE /api-keys/{keyId} Rules: secrets are returned exactly once at creation/rotation and stored hashed; scopes are least-privilege and mirrored from the operator's effective authorization — an API client can never exceed its owner's scope; insufficient_scope is returned as 403 auth.forbidden with error.details[].requiredScope.

API-3 — Uniform request/response envelope

Every operation in this document conforms to the global conventions block. This contract additionally guarantees: - GET responses expose ETag and honor If-None-Match → 304. - PATCH/PUT require If-Match. - POST commands require Idempotency-Key; a replay returns the original status, body, and Idempotency-Replayed: true; a replay with a divergent payload returns 409 idempotency.mismatch. - Errors always carry requestId; retryable is explicit on 429, 503, and transient 5xx.

API-4 — Asynchronous long-running operations

GET /operations/{operationId} → { "kind": "bulk_start|delete_project|export|deployment_upgrade", "status": "pending|running|succeeded|failed|cancelled", "progress": { "unitsDone", "unitsTotal" }, "resultRef": "...", "error": null } POST /operations/{operationId}:cancel → cooperative cancel. GET /operations/{operationId}/events → text/event-stream progress. Rules: any request that cannot complete inside 5 s returns 202 with an Operation; resultRef is a stable, scope-checked reference to the created/affected resources.

API-5 — Streaming surface

GET /api/v1/streams/{streamKind} (streamKind ∈ run_events | group_events | operation_events | audit_tail) Rules: text/event-stream with id:, event:, data: lines; Last-Event-ID replay is gap-free for at least 5 minutes; a heartbeat event every 15 s; 429 if a client exceeds its concurrent-stream quota; the stream never carries data the caller could not read synchronously.

API-6 — Webhooks and event subscriptions

Method Path Purpose
GET POST /webhooks
GET PATCH DELETE
POST /webhooks/{webhookId}:test Send a synthetic delivery
GET /webhooks/{webhookId}/deliveries Delivery log with response codes
POST /webhooks/{webhookId}/deliveries/{deliveryId}:redeliver Manual replay

Subscription body { "url": "https://...", "eventTypes": ["run.failed","gate.opened"], "secret": "returned-once", "filters": { "projectId": "prj_1" }, "active": true } Rules: every delivery is signed (X-PF-Signature: sha256=... over the raw body with a timestamp header to prevent replay); at-least-once delivery with exponential retry; deliveries are ordered per subscriptionId + objectId; a failing endpoint is auto-disabled after a configured threshold and notified to the owner.

API-7 — Bulk operations

POST /bulk-operations → { "operation": "create|update|delete|command", "resource": "projects|runs|test-cases", "items": [...], "atomic": false } Response 202 + Operation; results enumerate per-item succeeded|failed with an error per failed item. Rules: atomic: true rolls the whole batch back on any failure; batch size cap per resource (default 500) exceeded → 422.

API-8 — Dry run

Any state-changing operation accepts Dry-Run: true and returns 200 with { "dryRun": true, "effect": { ... }, "warnings": [] } and no persisted change. Dry-Run is honored on F-6 commands, F-4 bulk operations, F-12 exports, and F-15 upgrades.

API-9 — Rate limiting and quotas

GET /quotas → { "limits": [ { "scope": "client", "metric": "requests_per_minute", "limit": 600, "used": 120, "resetAt": "ISO-8601" } ] } Every response carries X-RateLimit-Limit/Remaining/Reset; 429 responses carry Retry-After and error.retryable: true. Rules: streaming connections and bulk operations are metered by their own metrics; quota breaches are recorded in the API activity audit.

API-10 — Capability discovery

GET /api/v1/capabilities → machine-readable list of enabled capabilities (features, optional subsystems, deployment-exposed surfaces) with the operation ids that implement each, so a client can adapt instead of probing. Rules: discovery reflects actual runtime enablement (e.g. an air-gapped deployment reports AI/voice capabilities as disabled).

API-11 — API activity audit

GET /api-audit?filter[clientId]=&filter[operationId]=&filter[outcome]=&cursor= → immutable record per call: actor, client, operation, target, outcome, latency, request id, and any scope denials. Rules: read-only; retained per the deployment's retention policy; secrets never appear in audit payloads.

API-12 — SDK / CLI generation and sandbox

GET /api/v1/sdk/{language} → generated client artifact reference (versioned alongside the contract). POST /sandbox/sessions → ephemeral, isolated installation-scoped sandbox with the same contract and reset-on-request semantics. POST /sandbox/sessions/{sessionId}:reset → wipe sandbox state. Rules: sandbox credentials are distinct from production credentials and sandbox operations can never target non-sandbox resources.

F-14: API Reuse and Extension Analysis

API-1 — Contract inventory snapshots

Method Path Purpose
POST /api-inventory/snapshots Capture a snapshot from the F-13 contract surface
GET /api-inventory/snapshots List snapshots (filter[apiVersion], filter[from], filter[to])
GET /api-inventory/snapshots/{snapshotId} Read snapshot metadata and counts
GET /api-inventory/snapshots/{snapshotId}/operations Enumerated operations with signature, scopes, resources
GET /api-inventory/diff?from=&to= Added / removed / changed operations

Rules: snapshots are immutable and read-only against F-13 — the analysis layer never mutates, deprecates, or publishes a contract.

API-2 — Demand registry

Method Path Purpose
GET /api-demand List registered demand items
POST /api-demand Register demand { "capability": "string", "consumer": "string", "area": "runs|projects|tests|...", "rationale": "string", "priority": "must|should|could" }
GET /api-demand/{demandId} Read
PATCH /api-demand/{demandId} Update status/rationale
DELETE /api-demand/{demandId} Withdraw (soft delete, retained for history)

API-3 — Reuse analysis requests and results

Method Path Purpose
POST /reuse-analyses Run analysis { "demandIds": [], "snapshotId": "snap_9", "areas": ["runs"] }
GET /reuse-analyses List analyses
GET /reuse-analyses/{analysisId} Read status, summary counts, coverage
GET /reuse-analyses/{analysisId}/findings Per-demand classification

Finding body

{ "demandId": "d_1", "classification": "reuse|extend|version|new",
  "matchedOperations": ["POST /runs/{runId}:retry"],
  "gap": "no per-stage retry on run groups",
  "confidence": 0.82,
  "conflicts": [ { "withOperation": "POST /run-groups/{id}:retry-failed", "kind": "overlap" } ],
  "recommendation": "extend" }

Rules: classification is one of the four fixed values — the analysis may recommend, it may not decide; low-confidence findings (confidence < 0.6) are marked needs_review; the engine is read-only against F-13 and produces no contract changes.

API-4 — Duplication and overlap findings

GET /duplication-findings?filter[area]=&filter[severity]= → pairs of near-identical or overlapping operations with similarity score, the differing aspects, and a recommended resolution (merge|alias|leave|deprecate_candidate). Rules: deprecate_candidate is advisory only — deprecation is F-13's lifecycle act, never F-14's.

API-5 — Coverage scoring

GET /api-coverage?area=&snapshotId= → { "area": "runs", "score": 0.78, "coveredDemand": 12, "totalDemand": 15, "uncoveredDemandIds": ["d_4","d_9"] } Rules: the score is a derived, reproducible function of demand + snapshot; it is stamped with snapshotId so historical scores remain explainable.

API-6 — Review and decision records

GET|POST /reuse-decisions → { "analysisId": "ra_1", "demandId": "d_1", "decision": "reuse|extend|version|new|reject", "decidedByUserId": "usr_7", "rationale": "string (>=20 chars)", "targetOperationId": "optional" } GET /reuse-decisions/{decisionId} → decision with the exact analysis revision it was based on. Rules: decisions are immutable; a superseding decision references the one it replaces; a decision never itself modifies a contract.

API-7 — Published reuse guidance

GET /reuse-guidance?area= → human- and machine-readable guidance: which operation to use for a capability, what is intentionally not offered, and the extension path for the remainder. POST /reuse-guidance:export → 202 + artifact reference for a guidance snapshot.

F-15: Deployment Options

API-1 — Deployment target catalog

GET /deployment-targets → supported targets with guarantees.

{ "id": "self_hosted_kubernetes", "name": "Self-hosted Kubernetes",
  "topologies": ["single_region_ha", "multi_region"],
  "sizingProfiles": ["s","m","l"],
  "guarantees": { "offlineCapable": true, "dataResidency": "operator_choice", "sla": "operator_defined" },
  "capabilities": { "ai": true, "voice": false, "notifications": true },
  "entitlements": ["enterprise"] }

GET /deployment-targets/{targetId} → full requirements: prerequisites, resource minimums, network/TLS/identity wiring, secret contract, supported upgrade paths. Rules: the catalog is the single source of truth for what is supported; a deployment that does not match a catalog entry is reported as unsupported with the deviating fields.

API-2 — Deployment records and provisioning

Method Path Purpose
POST /deployments Provision/bootstrap a deployment
GET /deployments List
GET /deployments/{deploymentId} Read status and health summary
PATCH /deployments/{deploymentId} Update metadata/labels
POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:decommission Tear down
DELETE /deployments/{deploymentId} Remove the record

Provision request { "targetId": "self_hosted_kubernetes", "topology": "single_region_ha", "sizingProfile": "m", "region": "eu-west-1", "configRevisionId": "cfg_2|null", "hardeningProfile": "baseline|hardened" } Response 202 + Operation with step-by-step progress. Rules: provisioning is idempotent per Idempotency-Key; decommission requires ?confirm=<deploymentName> and refuses while the deployment serves active runs unless force=true; the record is retained after decommission for audit.

API-3 — Installation manifest

GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/manifest → the machine-readable manifest describing the running installation: deployment id, target, topology, sizing, component versions, enabled capabilities, feature flags, data-residency placement, entitlement binding, config revision, and build identifiers. Rules: the manifest is the authoritative description of a running installation and is consumed by F-13 capability discovery; it is read-only via this API (changes flow through configuration revisions, API-4).

API-4 — Configuration and secrets

Method Path Purpose
GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/configuration Current effective configuration (secrets redacted)
PUT /deployments/{deploymentId}/configuration Apply a new configuration revision
GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/configuration/revisions Revision history
POST /deployments/{deploymentId}/configuration/revisions/{revisionId}:rollback Roll back configuration

Rules: secret values are never returned — only { "key": "...", "source": "vault|k8s-secret|env", "present": true }; configuration is validated against the target's schema before acceptance (422 with field-level details); applying a revision is a long-running operation when it requires a restart.

API-5 — Upgrade and rollback

POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:upgrade-plan → { "targetVersion": "1.8.0" } → compatibility report (blockers[], warnings[], estimatedDowntime, requiredBackups). POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:upgrade → 202 + Operation (requires a cleared plan id). POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:rollback → { "toVersion": "1.7.3", "reason": "string" } GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/upgrades → history with outcomes.

Rules: upgrade is refused (409 conflict.invalid_state) if the plan reports unresolved blockers; rollback requires a compatible backup and returns 422 when the schema migration is one-way; every upgrade/rollback is recorded immutably with the executing actor.

API-6 — Scaling, health, and parity

Method Path Purpose
POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:scale { "sizingProfile": "l", "componentOverrides": {} }
GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/health Component-level health with ok|degraded|down and dependency status
GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/parity Capability parity vs. the target's catalog guarantees
GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/drift Detected drift from the declared configuration/manifest

Rules: scaling is rejected if it would violate the target's data-residency or HA guarantees (422); drift findings are advisory and link to the exact deviating field; a down component reports its blast radius (which capabilities are unavailable).

API-7 — Backup and restore

POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:backup → { "scope": "full|config|data", "retentionDays": 30 } → 202 + Operation + backup id. GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/backups → list with verification status. POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:restore → { "backupId": "bkp_9", "confirm": true } → 202; restore is destructive and requires deployment:restore scope. Rules: every backup is integrity-verified and reports verified: true|false; restore into a non-empty deployment requires confirm=true and is refused while runs are active unless force=true.

API-8 — Observability and identity wiring

GET|PUT /deployments/{deploymentId}/observability → metrics/tracing/log sinks, sampling, retention. GET|PUT /deployments/{deploymentId}/identity → identity provider wiring (OIDC/SAML/LDAP), group-to-role mapping, and session policy. POST /deployments/{deploymentId}/identity:test → connection and mapping dry run.

Rules: identity changes are validated with a dry run before persistence; observability endpoints never expose credentials, only destination identifiers and health status.

API-9 — Cost estimation and data residency

POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:cost-estimate → { "sizingProfile", "targetId", "topology", "region", "usageAssumptions": { "runsPerDay", "avgRunMinutes" } } → line-item estimate with a timestamped, deterministic basis. GET /deployments/{deploymentId}/residency → where each data class (project, run telemetry, transcript/audio, audit, backups) is stored, with region and retention per class. Rules: residency answers must enumerate every data class — an unknown class is reported as unclassified rather than omitted; cost estimates never mutate deployment state.

API-10 — Air-gapped / offline delivery

GET /deployment-targets/{targetId}/offline-bundle → bundle manifest (version, checksums, size) for disconnected installation. POST /deployments/{deploymentId}:offline-import → 202 + Operation applying a signed bundle. Rules: bundles must be signature-verified; an unsigned or mismatched bundle is rejected with 422; an air-gapped deployment reports AI/voice capabilities as disabled through F-13 API-10 discovery rather than failing at call time.

F-16: Voice Reference Alignment

API-1 — Reference registration and lifecycle

Method Path Purpose
GET /voice-references List (filter[locale], filter[status], q)
POST /voice-references Register a target voice { "name", "locale", "description", "intendedUse" }
GET /voice-references/{referenceId} Read with sample and version summary
PATCH /voice-references/{referenceId} Update metadata (never audio)
POST /voice-references/{referenceId}:retire Retire
DELETE /voice-references/{referenceId} Delete (blocked if bound to a voice profile in use)

Rules: a reference must resolve to exactly one locale; retired references remain readable for audit but cannot be bound or used in new synthesis.

API-2 — Sample ingestion

POST /voice-references/{referenceId}/samples (multipart/form-data or application/json with a content reference) Request { "segments": [ { "uri": "...", "transcript": "string", "startMs", "endMs" } ], "consentAttestation": true } Response 202 + Operation. Rules: consentAttestation: true is mandatory — ingestion without it is rejected with 422 validation.field; unsupported codecs/sample rates are normalized and the transformation is reported in sample metadata; a sample failing integrity checks is stored as rejected with a diagnostic, never silently dropped.

API-3 — Preprocessing, alignment, and scoring

POST /voice-references/{referenceId}:align → { "sampleIds": [], "presetId": "default|null", "batch": true } → 202 + alignment job id. GET /voice-references/{referenceId}/alignments/{jobId} → job status and per-sample results:

{ "sampleId": "smp_1", "status": "pass|review|fail",
  "scores": { "alignment": 0.94, "consistency": 0.88, "quality": 0.91 },
  "diagnostics": [ { "code": "low_energy_segment", "atMs": 4120 } ] }

POST /voice-references/{referenceId}/alignments/{jobId}:cancel Rules: verdicts are objective and thresholded by the active preset; pass requires all scores at or above the preset thresholds; fail results are retained with diagnostics and are never counted toward a profile version; batch alignment is resumable and reports per-sample progress.

API-4 — Alignment settings and presets

GET|PUT /voice-alignment-settings → thresholds, normalization policy, consistency window, and default preset. GET|POST /voice-alignment-presets, GET|PATCH|DELETE /voice-alignment-presets/{presetId} Rules: changing thresholds never retroactively alters existing verdicts; a new verdict set is produced as a new alignment run tied to the preset version used.

API-5 — Reference profile versions

Method Path Purpose
POST /voice-references/{referenceId}/versions Compose an immutable profile version from passing samples
GET /voice-references/{referenceId}/versions List versions
GET /voice-reference-versions/{versionId} Read version with composition and scores
POST /voice-reference-versions/{versionId}:approve Approve for use
POST /voice-reference-versions/{versionId}:retire Retire

Compose request { "sampleIds": ["smp_1","smp_2"], "presetId": "default", "notes": "string" } Rules: composition accepts only samples with a pass verdict; versions are immutable (405 on edit); only an approved version may be bound (API-6) or used by F-10; approval requires the voice_reference:approve scope and records the approver immutably.

API-6 — Locale and Voice Companion binding

GET|PUT /voice-reference-bindings → mapping of locale → approved profile version, plus the resolution rule (exact locale → language fallback → platform default). GET /voice-reference-bindings/resolve?locale=en-GB → the exact profile version F-10 would use, with the resolution path taken. POST /voice-reference-bindings:validate → detects gaps, ambiguities, or bindings to retired versions. Rules: only approved versions are bindable (409 otherwise); an unresolved locale returns 404 with the attempted resolution chain so the failure is diagnosable; F-10 always resolves through this contract rather than selecting a profile itself.

API-7 — Drift and cross-sample consistency

GET /voice-references/{referenceId}/drift → comparison of the approved profile against fresh samples: per-sample scores, drift verdict (stable|drifting|diverged), and the samples responsible. POST /voice-references/{referenceId}/drift:check → run a check against newly ingested samples → 202 + Operation. GET /voice-references/{referenceId}/consistency → intra- and cross-sample consistency with outliers enumerated. Rules: diverged never mutates an approved version — it raises a review recommendation and optionally notifies per API-8; a new version must be explicitly composed and approved to take effect.

API-8 — Preview synthesis, notifications, and failures

POST /voice-reference-versions/{versionId}:preview-synthesis → { "text": "string (<=500 chars)", "format": "opus|mp3|wav" } → audio stream plus a preview id. GET /voice-references/{referenceId}/notifications → completion, approval, drift, and failure notifications for the reference owner. GET /voice-alignment-failures?filter[referenceId]= → diagnosable failure clusters with remediation hints. Rules: preview synthesis is permitted for non-approved versions (that is its purpose) but is watermarked/labelled preview: true and may never be routed to a live Voice Companion session.

API-9 — Search, import, and export

GET /voice-references:search?q=&locale=&status= → ranked search across references, versions, and samples with facets. POST /voice-references:export → 202 + artifact containing references, versions, and bindings (audio exported only when policy permits, with consent provenance attached). POST /voice-references:import → 202 + Operation; imported references arrive unapproved and must pass alignment and approval before binding. Rules: export excludes rejected samples by default; import of a reference whose locale conflicts with an existing binding does not auto-rebind — it returns a conflict report requiring explicit resolution.

API-10 — Role-gated access and audit

GET /voice-references/{referenceId}/access → caller's effective scopes (read, ingest, align, compose, approve, retire, bind). GET /voice-reference-audit?filter[referenceId]=&filter[objectKind]= → immutable trail covering registration, sample ingestion (with consent attestation references), alignment runs and verdicts, version composition, approvals, retirements, binding changes, drift checks, previews, and imports/exports — each with actor, timestamp, and preset/version identifiers. Read-only; no mutation endpoint exists.

Design Direction

Product Forge is an operator console, not a marketing site — its design direction is mission-control calm under load. The visual language is built on a dark-first, high-density canvas (a deep neutral base such as #0E1116 with layered surface tones) so that the primary signal in any view — run status, gate backlog, stage progress, failure severity — is the brightest thing on screen, and nothing competes with it. State is never carried by colour alone: a consistent, semantically-locked palette (running = active blue, succeeded = affirmative green, blocked/failed = alert red, paused/waiting = amber, archived = muted grey) is always paired with an icon, a text label, and an ARIA-announced status so the same meaning survives colour-blindness, low-vision, and screen-reader use. Typography is a two-role system: a compact, tabular-figure monospace for identifiers, timestamps, stage numbers, run IDs and telemetry (so columns align and scanability is preserved at 10k-project scale), and a humanist sans for prose, labels, and conversation — the voice of the AI companion (F-9) and the operator's own notes. Motion is functional, never decorative: skeletons on first paint, subtle transitions on state change, zero animation on continuous telemetry streams, and full prefers-reduced-motion honour. The mood is quiet confidence — dense but breathable, information-rich without being noisy, and always showing the operator what the system is doing, why it did it, and what they can do next. Every surface in F-1 through F-16 composes from this one language so a portfolio roll-up, a live stage timeline, a HIL gate decision, and a voice-driven answer all feel like the same product, whether they arrive on a 34-inch control-room display or a phone in an operator's pocket.

Components

Owned by F-8 (Dashboard UX Component System) unless the row names another feature. Components are presentation contracts only: a component renders or emits intent, never mutates domain state. Every command-emitting component hands its intent to the manual command surface owned by F-6 for preview, confirmation, and audit. Local ids (CMP-n) are scoped to this section and do not collide with the FR-/NFR-/US-/API- ranges owned elsewhere.

Component Layering

Tier Purpose Owner Rule
T0 — Design tokens Colour, spacing, type, elevation, motion, density F-8 Semantic tokens only; no raw hex in feature components
T1 — Primitives Single-purpose, unopinionated building blocks F-8 No domain vocabulary in names or props
T2 — Composed Layout, feedback, data-display, command-safety patterns F-8 Reusable across ≥2 features; no domain fetches
T3 — Feature components Domain-aware compositions for F-1 … F-16 Named per row Fetch only their own feature's published surface
T4 — Surfaces Route-level assemblies (console, wizard, mobile shell) Composed from T1–T3 One surface per journey, not per feature

Cross-Cutting Component Contracts

  • Rendering contract. Every T3 component declares the authoritative feature surface it reads. No component infers domain state from a sibling component; F-8 renders, features own.
  • Real-time contract. Live surfaces use CMP-90/CMP-91; on reconnect, stale renders are marked with CMP-68/CMP-253 until a fresh frame arrives. Animations respect CMP-102.
  • Command contract. No T3 component calls a mutation endpoint directly. It emits a command intent through CMP-85/CMP-86, is gated by CMP-88, confirmed per CMP-77/CMP-181, and echoed into the audit trail via CMP-190. This holds for keyboard, touch, chat-drafted (F-9), spoken (F-10), and mobile (F-11) origins identically.
  • State coverage contract. Every list/table ships CMP-60, every mutation ships CMP-74/CMP-75/CMP-77, every async dependency ships CMP-22/CMP-82, every network boundary ships CMP-76/CMP-61. No surface may render an unhandled empty, loading, or error state.
  • Accessibility contract. Status is always colour + icon + text + LiveRegionAnnouncer; CMP-72, CMP-3…CMP-10 expose labels, descriptions, and aria-invalid/aria-describedby; composite widgets implement full keyboard semantics; RTL parity is verified for every T2 component via CMP-101.

Data Models

Scope: canonical domain and persistence model for Product Forge (features F-1 … F-16). Entity schemas are grouped by the feature that owns their lifecycle. Other sections reference entities by name only and never redefine fields. Local ids (DM-n) are scoped to this section.

Conventions

Rule Definition
Identifier id is a system-generated opaque, lexicographically sortable identifier (ULID). Never reused, never recycled after deletion.
Timestamps createdAt, updatedAt, archivedAt, deletedAt are ISO-8601 UTC with millisecond precision.
Optimistic concurrency Every mutable entity carries revision (monotonic integer starting at 1). Any write supplies the expected revision; mismatch returns 409 conflict.stale_revision.
Ownership Every domain entity carries ownerId (principal reference) and, where authorization is scoped, projectId, portfolioId, or runGroupId. Missing scope means installation-wide.
Provenance Every mutable entity carries createdBy and updatedBy (actor references). Immutable entities carry auditRef pointing to the command/decision record that produced them.
Deletion Archived entities keep archivedAt and are hidden from default listings; permanently deleted entities keep a tombstone row (deletedAt set) so ids are never reused. Runs, decisions, test results, and audit records are immutable and are never deleted.
Sensitivity Fields marked [sensitive] are encrypted at rest, redacted from list responses, and excluded from telemetry. Fields marked [secret] are write-only and never returned.
Cross-feature rule A feature reads entities owned by other features through their published surfaces; only the owning feature writes. No shared mutable tables across feature boundaries.

Shared Value Objects

Id Name Shape Used by
VO-1 ActorRef { kind: user\|service\|system, id, displayName } all
VO-2 TimeWindow { from, to, timezone } schedules, reports, telemetry queries
VO-3 Money { amount, currency } cost estimation (F-15), budgets (F-7)
VO-4 ResourceRef { feature: "F-n", type, id, revision? } citations, audit, drafts
VO-5 PagedList { items[], nextCursor, total? } all list APIs
VO-6 LabelSet { key, value }[] with key uniqueness tags, labels
VO-7 Approval { decision: approved\|rejected, decidedBy, decidedAt, reason } gates, waivers, reviews
VO-8 Locale { language, region, script?, direction: ltr\|rtl } i18n, voice, references

Domain Entities

F-1 — Central Pipeline Control Plane

DM-1 Project — the lifecycle registry record at the centre of the control plane.

Field Type Constraints / Notes
id string PK
name string 1–200 chars, unique within owner scope (case-insensitive)
description string ≤ 4,000 chars
ownerId ActorRef required
status enum ProjectStatus draft \| active \| paused \| archived \| deleted
portfolioId string? FK → DM-21 Portfolio (null = unassigned)
pipelineBinding DM-5 PipelineBinding required once activated
configurationRevision integer incremented on any configuration change; basis for run reproducibility
tierAssignmentId string FK → DM-14 TierAssignment
tags LabelSet
visibility enum Visibility private \| team \| org
seedIdeaId string? FK → DM-10 SeedIdea
archivedAt / deletedAt timestamp? life-cycle markers

Invariants: status transitions follow draft → active → paused → active → archived → active; deleted is terminal and requires the project to be archived first. pipelineBinding is immutable once a run has executed against it; an upgrade creates a new binding revision.

DM-2 PipelineDefinition — a registered, named pipeline composed of ordered stages.

Field Type Notes
id string PK
name string unique
description string
ownerId ActorRef
currentVersionId string FK → DM-3 PipelineVersion
status enum DefinitionStatus draft \| published \| deprecated \| retired

DM-3 PipelineVersion — an immutable frozen snapshot of a definition.

Field Type Notes
id string PK
pipelineDefinitionId string FK → DM-2
versionNumber integer monotonic per definition
stageIds string[] ordered FK → DM-4
publishedAt timestamp
publishedBy ActorRef
checksum string content hash, basis for reproducibility
status enum VersionStatus published \| deprecated \| retired

Immutability: once published, a version is never mutated; changes create a new version.

DM-4 StageDefinition — a single stage in the 32-stage model.

Field Type Notes
id string PK
ordinal integer position in the pipeline (1-based)
name string
kind enum StageKind agent \| gate \| test \| deploy \| manual
entryConditions ConditionSet evaluated before dispatch
exitCriteria ConditionSet basis for stage success
hilGatePolicyId string? referenced when the stage is gated
defaultTimeoutSec integer
retryPolicyId string?

DM-5 PipelineBinding (value object embedded in DM-1).

Field Type Notes
pipelineDefinitionId string FK → DM-2

Relationship Map (cross-feature, read-only references)

From To Cardinality Rule
DM-1 Project DM-21 Portfolio many → 0..1 membership edge is DM-22; F-1 writes the FK, F-4 owns DM-21
DM-1 Project DM-5 PipelineBinding 1 → 1 immutable once a run has executed
DM-1 Project DM-14 TierAssignment 1 → 1 resolved via F-2 rules
DM-6 Run DM-1 Project many → 1 owns no project fields
DM-6 Run DM-25 RunGroup many → 0..1 parent link; F-5 owns DM-25
DM-7 StageRun DM-4 StageDefinition many → 1 plan fixes the version
DM-18 HILGate DM-19 GateDecision 1 → 0..n decisions are append-only
DM-18 HILGate DM-38 DecisionLedgerEntry 1 → 0..n present only when auto-decided
DM-30 ManualCommand DM-30 (all mutations) 1 → 1 every mutation traces to exactly one command or ledger entry
DM-45 Conversation DM-49 DraftCommand 1 → 0..n drafts are handed to F-6; never executed by F-9
DM-50 VoiceSession DM-45 Conversation many → 1 voice reuses F-9 conversation/grounding
DM-65 TestRun DM-6 Run many → 0..1 stage-bound runs only
DM-67 QualityGate DM-7 StageRun many → many gate verdict consumed by stage advancement (F-3)
DM-71 ApiContract (any feature) 1 → many contracts describe operations; they own no domain state
DM-81 ApiInventorySnapshot DM-72 ApiVersion 1 → many read-only projection (F-14)
DM-90 DeploymentRecord DM-1…DM-114 1 → 0..n one installation scopes all domain rows
DM-102 VoiceProfile DM-48 Citation many → many spoken citations resolved from cited objects

Retention & Lifecycle Summary

Entity group Retention Deletion semantics
DM-1, DM-21, DM-25 (registries) until archived, then indefinitely until permanent delete soft delete with tombstone
DM-6…DM-8, DM-17…DM-20 (execution) run retention window (configurable, default 1 year) immutable; never deleted
DM-19, DM-32, DM-38 (decisions/audit) audit retention (default 7 years) immutable; never deleted
DM-40…DM-44 (UX system) current + prior published version old themes retired, not deleted
DM-45…DM-49 (conversation) per user retention setting (default 90 days) user-initiated; drafts expire with conversation
DM-50…DM-54, DM-102…DM-114 (voice) audio ephemeral ≤ 24h; transcripts standard user-controlled; sensitive fields redacted on export
DM-55…DM-59 (mobile) until device revoked revoked devices drop caches immediately
DM-60…DM-70 (tests) per project retention results immutable; definitions versioned
DM-71…DM-80 (API) active + deprecated versions until retirement date published versions immutable
DM-81…DM-87 (analysis) rolling 12-month snapshot window derived; safe to prune
DM-88…DM-101 (deployment) installation lifetime records superseded, not deleted
DM-91 SecretReference until rotation/revocation value never persisted in any DM

Open Questions (data model)

  1. OQ-DM-1: Should DM-17 TelemetryEvent retention be per-project configurable, or fixed by F-15 deployment policy? Currently modelled as a run-level window — needs an owner decision.
  2. OQ-DM-2: DM-22 PortfolioMembership enforces one portfolio per project; if the user later wants multi-portfolio membership, the constraint must be relaxed by the user, not by this model.
  3. OQ-DM-3: DM-105 VoiceProfileVersion depends on DM-19-style approvals (VO-7) — the approval workflow owner is not yet assigned between F-16 and a future approvals feature.
  4. OQ-DM-4: Should DM-83 ReuseFinding reference a demand only, or also a proposed new API contract? Today it references demand only; extending it is additive and non-breaking.
